{"parentUuid":"14771b35-09cb-4abe-9bd7-03b866c908b6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"74a006da-e2d3-4f09-8619-d25d7592b2b7","agentId":"a76e037eedc47105d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CRCpfhTDisH47qQvrk7gob","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Market and economics\n2\t\n3\tSettled facts on the price/sell mechanism, the 2026-08-15 hinge balance change, crop-mix\n4\teconomics (carrot/tomato/egg/melon), simulator parity, pen geometry, and shed/wheat-churn defects.\n5\tFull detail for every line below is in `docs/history/`, named per line.\n6\t\n7\t## Settled\n8\t\n9\t- `market_price(item, inventory, params)` is a pure function of inventory, monotone decreasing above `I0 = 10,000` (`reference/kaggriculture.py:177-191`); sales at the $1 floor do not raise market inventory — the floor is an infinite sink. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n10\t- `FEED` consumes exactly 1 WHEAT per animal per day from the shed (`kaggriculture.py:505-512`), so wheat is a consumable as well as a market product. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n11\t- `_process_market` drains ONE shared market object per unit in lockstep between both seats (`kaggriculture.py:544-547`); every unit either seat sells lowers the other's price. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n12\t- Post-hinge price curve (2026-08-15): WHEAT ranges $19-$60 across -1200..+1000 surplus and never crashes; TOMATO hits $5,004 at -1200 deficit (hinge, quadratic below T); EGG is the only animal product that cannot be crashed, holding ~$38 at +1000 surplus. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n13\t- Units above I0 needed to reach the $1 floor: WOOL 59, STRAWBERRY 62, MILK 76, MELON 158, WHEAT effectively unfloodable (log curve, T=400). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n14\t- Season drain by product (2026-08-15, 8 shops with replacement): WHEAT 525/never floors, EGG 228/never floors, WOOL 228/floors at 59 units excess, MILK 327/floors at 76, STRAWBERRY 426/floors at 62, MELON 30/floors at 158 with no shop carrying it at all. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n15\t- Melon is a 3-day race d11-d14 on seed 3: price climbs to $271 as the town eats it before harvest, then crashes to $81 once both farms' melon lands; strawberry peaks $216 on d20 (+80% over base) then collapses to $59 by d29 as our own harvest lands. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n16\t- Realised prices, 12 recent ladder games: our wins sell MILK at $205/unit and STRAWBERRY at $182/unit; our losses sell the same products at $73 and $78; wheat is *better* in the losses ($49 vs $45). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n17\t- Pre-fix, both sell throttles were keyed to the tape's own production (we plant 77 tiles to its 45): on the seed-11 handover fixture 331 units were destroyed, shed pinned at its 100 cap from day 17. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n18\t- `PRICE_AWARE_SELL_CAP` on the handover fixture: $71,976/331 destroyed (OFF) vs $75,866/82 destroyed (ON), +$3,890 and -75% destroyed; read as neutral on the arena pool ($103,932 -> $102,095, win 94.4% both ways) because the pool was 50% mirror. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n19\t- `RESERVATION_SELL_CAP`: mean bank 62,868 -> 64,667 (+2.9%), units destroyed 86-165 -> 39-107 (about a third fewer). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n20\t- Across 14 real ladder losses, re-pricing only our own sales downward turns 12 of 14 into wins (recoverable revenue $7k-$33k/game); WOOL alone recovers +$135,250 summed, WHEAT and FERTILIZER recover $0. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n21\t- Optimal sell fractions from that sweep: WOOL 0.7x (best in 7/14 games), MILK 0.5x (best in 4/14), STRAWBERRY 0.7x (best in 6/14), WHEAT and FERTILIZER 1.0x with no gain ever. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n22\t- Lever correlation with money, 124 seats/62 games: sell_orders +0.384, feed +0.359, harvest +0.334, care +0.327, buy_product +0.255 (we sit at the 49th percentile against a top-quintile of 1,156 — the one lever with headroom), plant -0.050. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n23\t- Against `vs_tapes.py` we lose 4/4 to `oldleader` (margins -$8,982 to -$4,268, seeds 11/101/202/303) while beating Seb's own tape by +$98,483 on seed 11 — money against a tape runs 2-3x a real ladder game. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n24\t- 2026-08-15 organiser balance change buffs CARROT (+26% of games), TOMATO (+50%), EGG (+22%) via a hinge undersupply curve; requires `kaggle-environments >= 1.32.7`, not the `>= 1.32.2` `CLAUDE.md` stated at the time. — `history/balance_change_eggs_tomatoes_carrots_2026-08-15.md`\n25\t- Monte Carlo of the hinge mechanism (`price = base*(1+below_target*hinge(x/T))`, `hinge(u)=u+8*max(0,u-1)^2`) gives P(fires by d29, zero production) = CARROT 25.8%, TOMATO 53.4%, EGG 22.4% against the organiser's announced 26%/50%/22% — the model is validated. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n26\t- Paired sweep, `WHEAT_TILES_TO_CARROT` 0/8/28, 12 opponents, seeds 1300-1302, 72 boards/arm: arm 8 wins 83.3% (median margin delta +906), arm 28 wins 77.8% (median margin delta -1,883, sign test p<0.001) — the \"premium\" carrot pays LESS per unit than the wheat it replaces in the median game. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n27\t- Conditional tomato/carrot play (2+ relevant shops visible by d12, ~21-26% of games): tomato d29 median jumps to $216 (from $70 with 0 relevant shops), carrot to $81 (from $43) — expected value of a gated tomato play is ~$900/game mean, concentrated as ~$3,600 in games it fires. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n28\t- Corrected melon release value (seed 11, tape kawashigi): the shipped agent is nearly optimal already, leaving +$1,353 on the table on melon and +$5,653 total across all six products — not the -$11,929 an earlier broken trickle model printed. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n29\t- SUPERSEDED: `carrot_swap_2026-08-12.md`'s arms — two independent seed sets (1300-1303, 1306-1309), 384 games, `WHEAT_TILES_TO_CARROT=1` loses 10.4 and 7.8 win-rate points against 0 against every named opponent; `CARROT_SWAP` stayed False and no longer exists in the code (checked 2026-08-15); a count-based `CARROT_TILES=4`-from-day-20 was also a null (-1.1pp over 768 games) and was deleted. — `history/carrot_swap_2026-08-12.md`\n30\t- `carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md` (120 seats replayed with `record_fills`): CARROT sold by 94.2% of seats (median 15 units), but WHEAT nets $33.83/tile-day season-long against CARROT's $29.79 (fertilized: $52.06 vs $42.59) — verdict is no general carrot increase, carrot only pays as an end-of-season closer on tiles too short for wheat/melon. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n31\t- Carrot closing price correlates r=0.90 with the number of PET_CAFE/FARMERS_MARKET instances drawn (0 instances: $33-37 close; 3 instances: $56-69); TOMATO and EGG were sold by literally 0 of 120 leader seats, the genuinely empty books. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n32\t- 87 recorded ladder tapes' closing inventory: MELON always finishes with a surplus (+38 to +158, no shop ever carries it, 29-unit/season appetite) and FERTILIZER always finishes with a surplus (+201 to +493, no shop, no drain) — every board over-plants both, winners included; WHEAT's closing surplus is negative in all 87 tapes (-386 to -1,356), carried by 5 of 8 shop types. — `history/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md`\n33\t- Real plant counts (corrected from BUY_SEED order quantities, which re-post every turn): WHEAT 138, STRAWBERRY 36, MELON 19, CARROT 6, identical across five checked games — the earlier \"136 melon vs their 19\" claim was an artifact of counting orders, not plants. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n34\t- WHEAT vs CARROT per-cycle mechanics (`reference/kaggriculture.py`): WHEAT $10 seed/4-day cycle/8 plantings-season/28 unfertilized yield-per-tile vs CARROT $20 seed/3-day cycle/11 plantings-season/29 unfertilized yield-per-tile — CARROT needs 38% more cycles and costs 2x per planting for near-identical raw yield. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n35\t- Two 2026-08-13 ladder losses share one shape: `92550780` lost $69,881 to $77,275 while running 8 sheep against the opponent's 4 and shipping a fraction of the wool they did (28 units/$6,738 vs 115 units/$28,158); `92562037` lost $129,161 to $133,724 as a near-mirror where the one real divergence was melon (both sides plant 19 tiles once corrected). — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n36\t- `_daily_refresh_animals` removes an animal after two consecutive unfed days (`kaggriculture.py:804`); `92566708` lost 12 of 14 animals days 10-13 ($5,200 stock plus every remaining milk/wool unit for 17 days) while the shed held zero wheat days 8-12 and the bank sat at $1-$75-$22. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n37\t- CONTAMINATED: `LATE_SELL_BIAS`'s original measurement (pool win 14.6%->27.1%, money $16,146->$24,535, 2026-08-09) — `arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md` found `run_arena` drove one agent object through every match, and a fresh-agent-per-match re-run of the identical config gets $102,886/100% win against the contaminated $5,774/6.2%, a 17.8x gap; direction may still hold but the magnitude was never re-measured clean. — `history/fable_market_shape_2026-08-09.md`, `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n38\t- The shipped artifact was never on the contaminated path: `scripts/gate_pool.py` rebuilds the agent per job and measured clean at 94.4% win / $108,180 mean against the 9-member COMPETITIVE pool, seeds 700-705, both seatings, 108 games. — `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n39\t- Turning off every solved route from d12 costs ~4% on three-quadrant arms (straw_q3 -$5,906, melon_q3 -$7,539) while the mix barely moves (wheat tile count shifts by at most 3); the greedy alone runs a season in 8-25s against ~2 hours for the full solve, making it usable as a stage-1 mix ranker. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n40\t- Our routing beats tetsuya's own on his own schedule with zero precomputed routes: $62,365 against his recorded $55,330 (+$7,035) — the gap to #1 is the crop/herd mix (stage 1), not execution. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n41\t- Herd-split scoring fix (scored at his decisions, not final census) moved match rate 11.5% -> 55.8% -> 65.6% -> 77.9% purely by correcting the scoring method and the SHEEP-first opening floor (unanimous in 78/78 games), before any new rule; a milk-count threshold classifier (`MILK_SHOPS_FOR_A_BIG_HERD=2`) then pushed it to 88.5% (1051/1187), 21/21 two-milk-shop games going high-cow with zero false positives. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`, `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-20.md`\n42\t- The prior \"opponent sheep count predicts our split\" lead was pseudo-replication (213 placements over ~50 games); re-measured at the game level the gap collapses to -0.05 to -0.32, noise. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-20.md`\n43\t- TOMATO is gated at `TOMATO_DRAIN_SHOPS_NEEDED=2`: 0 of 34 games with fewer than two tomato-draining shops ever planted a tomato tile. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n44\t- Route/mix coupling: a strawberry-cap change measured -$10,553 against stale routes and +$4,022 once re-solved for it; a melon arm with NO crop change at all showed +$5,615 purely from route staleness; 2 added geese lost -$62,866 against stale routes and -$63,417 re-solved (not rescued). — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n45\t- Geese via `swap_in_geese` stayed broken even matched to geese-solved routes ($96,766 vs $97,317 cow-solved, both far below the $156,083-$160,183 no-geese baselines); geese from a schedule that natively contains coops (no swap) were harmless on 9/14 boards, going 6W-3L. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n46\t- SUPERSEDED: `market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`'s +$465-mean four-quadrant number — `q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`'s corrected measurement is -$716 mean, se $2,837, not distinguishable from zero on 11 seeds; the live-routing handicap alone (`shipped_live` vs `shipped`) costs -$1,873 and 2 wins, separable from the parcel/herd question. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`\n47\t- Market-as-one-token (tetsuya, 78 games, 56,082 seat-turns): 80.7% of turns carry no market action; 346 distinct market bundles total, top 100 cover 98.5% — the case for one categorical market-bundle head instead of nine independent heads. — `history/the_market_is_one_token_not_nine_heads_2026-08-20.md`\n48\t- Parity restored against kaggle-environments 1.32.6 (2026-08-09): four rules fixed (movement onto LOCKED tiles allowed, DROP/PICKUP/PLACE resolve before the LOCKED guard, BUY_* fails once shed is full, shops unlock WITH replacement capped at `MAX_SHOP_INSTANCES`); an all-distinct 8-shop draw has probability 0.24%, so duplicates are the norm on every parity seed tested. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n49\t- The tape fixture `tests/test_tape_parity.py` had recorded `EXPECTED_BANK=$147,178` from `module_version 1.32.3`; replaying the identical tape into installed 1.32.6 banks $92,313 instead — the test now compares live against the installed env rather than a stale recording. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n50\t- `run_arena` passed one agent OBJECT into every match instead of a factory (task #54, 2026-08-09): three consecutive matches through one object on seed 40 vs `pass` banked $114,028, then $0, then $0 — invalidation, not degradation; fix is `AgentFactory`, built once per match. — `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n51\t- Pen-placement geometry is not a lever, confirmed a third time on two fresh loss episodes (`91229682`, `91231728`): our walk-per-work-op ratio is BETTER than the opponent's in both (1.028 vs 1.118; 1.210 vs 1.445) despite our pens sitting measurably farther from the shed (mean shed-dist 2.86 vs 1.21; 2.19 vs 1.57). — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n52\t- A pre-existing \"1.86 vs 2.93\" ring-depth figure does not reproduce under Manhattan (3.25/1.90) or Euclidean (2.69/1.50) centroid metrics tried at every 2-day checkpoint; same direction, different magnitude — cite 3.25/1.90 going forward. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n53\t- The shed-discard problem was overstated ~15x by counting `held_at_dusk - shed_at_dawn` as the discard: instrumenting `_drop_inventories_to_shed` directly gives 7 units lost on the canonical full season (against a $55,837 bank), not the 105-137 the delta method reported; the 123-unit figure is real only on the handover-11 fixture, which is not the scored surface. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n54\t- `FEED_RESERVE_YIELDS_TO_OVERFLOW` had no day gate and fired from day 0 against a four-animal herd (day 14: OFF wheat=11/$63.0, ON wheat=21/$2,478.0); fixed by gating both guards to `day >= FEED_DAYS_FROM_DAY` (16), confirmed red again on revert. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n55\t- Releasing the feed reserve to cut discards is NOT a clean win across 7 seed/mode combinations: discards got worse in 5 of 7, money worse in 4 of 7, including full-season seed 12 at -$13,304 for 39 fewer units discarded. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n56\t- The wheat churn is a buy-side day-budget defect, not sell-side: the field buys 266-392 wheat units/season, we bought 1,099 (66-game average); `FEED_BUY_CEILING_DAYS=2` cuts season purchases 1,800->797 (-56%), keeps bank at parity (+$573 vs unfixed) and produces zero herd drops, matching the unfixed safety record. — `history/fable_the_wheat_churn_2026-08-09.md`\n57\t- `FEED_BUY_CEILING_DAYS=1` (one day's true consumption) is a worse fix: it cuts purchases to 393 but starves the herd through a separate delivery bottleneck (seed 11 day 15: 16-19 idle wheat in the shed while only 7 of 20 animals get fed; herd drops 20->14 by day 17). — `history/fable_the_wheat_churn_2026-08-09.md`\n58\t\n59\t- The derived sell rule has one free game-theoretic dial, `contest`: `1.0` is the single-player optimum, `0.5` the two-player split, and lower values sell sooner; both `market` and `town` are `shared: True` in the observation, so the rule needs no opponent order visible to compute. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n60\t- First local peak of revenue by product (CONTEST=1.0): STRAWBERRY peaks at 224 units/$26,880 season revenue, MELON at 88 units/$15,748, WHEAT at 276 units/$6,900 — \"saturation\" must be read at the first local peak, since the $1 floor makes revenue rise again forever with volume; a first version of this table searched globally and wrongly called wheat/carrot/tomato/melon/egg/fertilizer all saturating at 3,000 units. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`\n61\t- CORRECTED: geese are NOT an exception to saturation. An earlier claim that goose herds scale without limit came from `revenue_rate` computing herd output as `events * max_held` (max_held caps the unharvested buffer, not per-event yield, `kaggriculture.py:813`), overstating every herd 4-6x; corrected per-head daily output flattens at COW $1,164 (16+ head), SHEEP $897 (16+), GOOSE $246 (8+) — a cow herd's flat ceiling is 4.7x a goose herd's. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`\n62\t- Fertilizer is species-independent with zero drain (`kaggriculture.py:818` sets `fertilizer_available=True` for every animal tile daily, no shop and no `TOWN_CENTER_PRODUCTS` entry carries it): marginal price runs $90/unit at 50 sold down to $1 at 500 sold, cumulative revenue plateaus at $25,052 — every one of 87 recorded ladder tapes closes with fertilizer between $1 and $20 of a $100 base. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`, `history/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md`\n63\t- Season absorption differs sharply between env versions: WHEAT drain 635 units (1.32.2) vs 525 (1.32.6), MELON 140 vs 30 — `kagg/search/chains.py`'s `DRAIN_UNITS` still held the 1.32.2 column as of 2026-08-09 and overstated the market's worth by 1.13x (carrot) to 4.67x (melon), $308,160 believed vs a real $207,180, though stage 1 had not yet shipped in `submission/main.py` at that point so no ladder game was cost by it. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n64\t- Two-way design (6 tape opponents x 6 seeds, same agent/schedule/code, 36 seasons): final bank ranges from $16,888 to $163,036 with zero code changes; MILK realised price is board-driven (mean spread across seeds, opponent fixed: 88.5) and WOOL is opponent-driven (mean spread across opponents, seed fixed: 73.1) — WOOL is sold by 1 of 8 shops (thin demand, opponent volume dominates) while MILK is in 3 of 8 (the draw dominates). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n65\t- The \"high-value produce sits in hands at dusk, not the shed\" claim was false: `kagg/analysis/fills.py` on seeds 13/17/29/41 shows 100-109% of everything produced gets sold, 0 left in shed or hands at season end; the false reading came from comparing raw counts across different herd sizes and sampling at dusk (flow's max numerator, min denominator). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n66\t- A hand-turn census once reported \"idle 0.0%\" because the env's idle op is named `PASS`, not `IDLE`; the real split is 24% idle, 42% walking, ~34% working. — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n67\t- Shop-draw probability of never carrying a product in 8 draws with replacement: WHEAT 0.2%, STRAWBERRY 2.3%, MILK 2.3%, WOOL 34.4%, MELON always (no shop stocks it) — the numeric seed is hidden from observations but `town[\"unlocked_shops\"]` is `shared: True` and fully observable every turn. — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n68\t- The forum claim \"multiply base price by shop count\" is half right: shop count sets the sustainable drain *rate*, not the price — milk with three shops still floors after 76 units of excess, which is why our own milk repeatedly closed at $1-$5 despite the claim saying it \"sells above 160 most of the season.\" — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n69\t- Herd totals across 14 downloaded games cluster at (COW,SHEEP) = (6,8) in 9 games, (10,4) in 5; the four 13-animal (not 14) games are exactly the four games with a starved-animal escape, all from the same tile/day pattern: fed d8, missed d9 and d10, gone at d11 h00, a $400 replacement purchased the same day for an animal that never produced a unit. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n70\t- Melon front-load fix (`NO_DRAIN_THRESHOLD`, sell a no-drain product the day it arrives rather than trickling): test `tests/test_a_no_drain_product_is_sold_the_day_it_arrives.py` is red before the fix, green after, red again on revert; the race it fixes exists because recorded opponents dump 114-120 melons into the same shared book. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n71\t- The market-depth table computed three independent ways (base×shop-count arithmetic, units-to-$1-floor, and drain-with-price-at-N) agree on direction: WHEAT and EGG never floor; WOOL, MILK and STRAWBERRY are shallow (59-76 units to floor) and simultaneously the highest-value and easiest-to-destroy products. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`, `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`, `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n72\t- A price-neutral offer size does not track price level: 7 units of strawberry ($120 base) or 53 of fertilizer ($100 base) both hold the book flat — a seven-fold spread in tolerable offer size at similar base prices. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n73\t- \"Less, not slower\": across 56 real-loss cases, downscaling the sell (fewer units, same shape) gains +$254,293 total (best in 16 of 56); spreading (same units, even cadence) gains +$87,343 (best in 16); both together gain +$252,611 (best in 17) — cutting plus spreading adds nothing over cutting alone, except for WOOL, which is a pure volume problem (one episode: $5,278 observed vs $23,794 at 0.7x volume). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n74\t- Two traps caught twice: money is not the ladder objective (Seb's own sell mix earned more money on every seed and still lost 8/8 head-to-head, because ceding wool let the opponent earn more than we gained); and a replayed tape does not react to being beaten, so absolute money against a tape runs 2-3x a real ladder game. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n75\t- Shop-to-product map (units/day per instance): WHEAT is carried by 5 shop types (bakery/pizza/brunch/ice-cream/farmers, 6 each), STRAWBERRY by 4, MILK by 3, CARROT/TOMATO/EGG by 2 each, WOOL by 1 (yarn store), MELON by none — shops unlock one per three days, drawn with replacement, capped at `MAX_SHOP_INSTANCES=8`. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n76\t- The shipped plan's season sell totals against daily drain: FERTILIZER (1,487 sells against 0 drain/day) and MELON (120 sells against 1/day, 4x the season's absorption) are the two lines that break the budget; CARROT, MILK, STRAWBERRY and WOOL absorb within 1.1-14.7 days. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n77\t- Melon/strawberry tile-swap payoff is CONDITIONAL, not universal: one 2026-08-13 loss (`92562037`, 5 strawberry-carrying shop instances, drained 895, closed $187 above base) says a melon tile converted to strawberry pays several times more; another same-day loss (`92550780`, 1 instance, drained 179, closed $3, floored) says the swap buys nothing because that town wanted wool and carrot instead — a global sweep over `melon_day`/`melon_shift` averages both regimes and reports a wash, the expected result of asking a global question about a conditional quantity. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n78\t- EGG closed above base in all five games checked directly ($59-$88, town draining up to 1,074 units) with zero geese run in any of the five — EGG was unsellable at all when the original goose flag was measured (see `animals_and_the_herd.md`). — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n79\t- `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` correctly held cows at cap 6 on a three-YARN_STORE board (`92550780`) but `_herd_split`'s early return is forbidden from growing `want_total` beyond the schedule's own count, so the rule's own reference table (COW 6, SHEEP 12, total 18) landed as COW 6, SHEEP 8, total 14 — four animals short, because the early return skips the drain-aware greedy branch below it. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n80\t\n81\t## Open\n82\t\n83\t- Whether the shop draw is seed-only or agent-dependent: the weed-spawn RNG draws before the shop unlock and depends on which tiles are empty, so agent play shifts the shop sequence (seed 13/29 examples where PASS-vs-PASS and real play diverge). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n84\t- Whether the q4 four-quadrant branch generalises off its 15.7%-of-seeds early-yarn trigger population — not tested outside seeds 0-69 and a frozen mirror opponent. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`\n85\t- Carrot has no predictor found yet at the per-game level: 9 games at drain counts 1-3 are evenly spread and non-monotone in whether carrot gets planted. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n86\t- Whether the fast-sim 300/300 parity gate has been re-run since the 2026-08-15 1.32.7 hinge fix landed in `kagg/sim/market.py` and `kagg/sim/jax_sim.py` — no doc names a fresh full-episode run since, while `CLAUDE.md` states parity PASSES as fact with no version qualifier. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n87\t- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT=True` (always-nearest pen siting) was reverted for being penalised by a cell-count-resemblance-to-tape proxy metric, not a bank/win-rate test; its own docstring calls this unresolved and no bank/win-rate re-test has been run. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n88\t- A wheat 2-4-unit close-of-season residue in the shed is unexplained; the regression test was relaxed to `<= 3` rather than the defect being fixed. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n89\t\n90\t## Contradictions\n91\t\n92\t- Melon-swap rule direction: root `RETRACTIONS.md` item 7 (960-game population sweep, outside `docs/`) has the shipped rule (`MELON_YIELD_OPPONENT_MELON=7`) narrowly BEATING it off (`=99`), 68.1% vs 67.9% win. `when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`'s model and `work_to_be_done_2026-08-16.md` §4's direct A/B (-$17,297/game, 66.7% vs 0%) both say keeping melon beats swapping. Not resolved. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`, `history/work_to_be_done_2026-08-16.md`\n93\t- `LATE_SELL_BIAS`'s original magnitude (14.6%->27.1% win, $16,146->$24,535) vs the state-leak-corrected re-run of the identical config (6.2%->100% win, $5,774->$102,886) — a 17.8x gap on money and win rate alike; neither file reconciles the two. — `history/fable_market_shape_2026-08-09.md`, `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n94\t- Four-quadrant herd size: `q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`'s brief and code target COW 6/SHEEP 12=18 for the 2026-08-14 100-tile field; the same-day `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` measurement over 74 Kawashigi seats finds four-quadrant herds run 13-16, never 18. Both are stated of different players; not reconciled into one target number. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`, `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n95\t- STALE: `when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`'s headline total (+$12,136 from spreading sales evenly) compares against an infeasible baseline; the real release value is +$5,653 (see the melon release line above), and its own falsifiable prediction (`MELON_YIELD_OPPONENT_MELON=99` should beat `=7`) was tested on 960 games and falsified. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n96\t"}]},"uuid":"8fd39d4d-e0c8-400f-bb8e-91d15cba7310","timestamp":"2026-08-24T11:38:30.133Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/docs/market_and_economics.md","content":"# Market and economics\n\nSettled facts on the price/sell mechanism, the 2026-08-15 hinge balance change, crop-mix\neconomics (carrot/tomato/egg/melon), simulator parity, pen geometry, and shed/wheat-churn defects.\nFull detail for every line below is in `docs/history/`, named per line.\n\n## Settled\n\n- `market_price(item, inventory, params)` is a pure function of inventory, monotone decreasing above `I0 = 10,000` (`reference/kaggriculture.py:177-191`); sales at the $1 floor do not raise market inventory — the floor is an infinite sink. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- `FEED` consumes exactly 1 WHEAT per animal per day from the shed (`kaggriculture.py:505-512`), so wheat is a consumable as well as a market product. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n- `_process_market` drains ONE shared market object per unit in lockstep between both seats (`kaggriculture.py:544-547`); every unit either seat sells lowers the other's price. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n- Post-hinge price curve (2026-08-15): WHEAT ranges $19-$60 across -1200..+1000 surplus and never crashes; TOMATO hits $5,004 at -1200 deficit (hinge, quadratic below T); EGG is the only animal product that cannot be crashed, holding ~$38 at +1000 surplus. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n- Units above I0 needed to reach the $1 floor: WOOL 59, STRAWBERRY 62, MILK 76, MELON 158, WHEAT effectively unfloodable (log curve, T=400). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- Season drain by product (2026-08-15, 8 shops with replacement): WHEAT 525/never floors, EGG 228/never floors, WOOL 228/floors at 59 units excess, MILK 327/floors at 76, STRAWBERRY 426/floors at 62, MELON 30/floors at 158 with no shop carrying it at all. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- Melon is a 3-day race d11-d14 on seed 3: price climbs to $271 as the town eats it before harvest, then crashes to $81 once both farms' melon lands; strawberry peaks $216 on d20 (+80% over base) then collapses to $59 by d29 as our own harvest lands. — `history/market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`\n- Realised prices, 12 recent ladder games: our wins sell MILK at $205/unit and STRAWBERRY at $182/unit; our losses sell the same products at $73 and $78; wheat is *better* in the losses ($49 vs $45). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Pre-fix, both sell throttles were keyed to the tape's own production (we plant 77 tiles to its 45): on the seed-11 handover fixture 331 units were destroyed, shed pinned at its 100 cap from day 17. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- `PRICE_AWARE_SELL_CAP` on the handover fixture: $71,976/331 destroyed (OFF) vs $75,866/82 destroyed (ON), +$3,890 and -75% destroyed; read as neutral on the arena pool ($103,932 -> $102,095, win 94.4% both ways) because the pool was 50% mirror. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- `RESERVATION_SELL_CAP`: mean bank 62,868 -> 64,667 (+2.9%), units destroyed 86-165 -> 39-107 (about a third fewer). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Across 14 real ladder losses, re-pricing only our own sales downward turns 12 of 14 into wins (recoverable revenue $7k-$33k/game); WOOL alone recovers +$135,250 summed, WHEAT and FERTILIZER recover $0. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Optimal sell fractions from that sweep: WOOL 0.7x (best in 7/14 games), MILK 0.5x (best in 4/14), STRAWBERRY 0.7x (best in 6/14), WHEAT and FERTILIZER 1.0x with no gain ever. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Lever correlation with money, 124 seats/62 games: sell_orders +0.384, feed +0.359, harvest +0.334, care +0.327, buy_product +0.255 (we sit at the 49th percentile against a top-quintile of 1,156 — the one lever with headroom), plant -0.050. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Against `vs_tapes.py` we lose 4/4 to `oldleader` (margins -$8,982 to -$4,268, seeds 11/101/202/303) while beating Seb's own tape by +$98,483 on seed 11 — money against a tape runs 2-3x a real ladder game. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- 2026-08-15 organiser balance change buffs CARROT (+26% of games), TOMATO (+50%), EGG (+22%) via a hinge undersupply curve; requires `kaggle-environments >= 1.32.7`, not the `>= 1.32.2` `CLAUDE.md` stated at the time. — `history/balance_change_eggs_tomatoes_carrots_2026-08-15.md`\n- Monte Carlo of the hinge mechanism (`price = base*(1+below_target*hinge(x/T))`, `hinge(u)=u+8*max(0,u-1)^2`) gives P(fires by d29, zero production) = CARROT 25.8%, TOMATO 53.4%, EGG 22.4% against the organiser's announced 26%/50%/22% — the model is validated. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n- Paired sweep, `WHEAT_TILES_TO_CARROT` 0/8/28, 12 opponents, seeds 1300-1302, 72 boards/arm: arm 8 wins 83.3% (median margin delta +906), arm 28 wins 77.8% (median margin delta -1,883, sign test p<0.001) — the \"premium\" carrot pays LESS per unit than the wheat it replaces in the median game. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n- Conditional tomato/carrot play (2+ relevant shops visible by d12, ~21-26% of games): tomato d29 median jumps to $216 (from $70 with 0 relevant shops), carrot to $81 (from $43) — expected value of a gated tomato play is ~$900/game mean, concentrated as ~$3,600 in games it fires. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n- Corrected melon release value (seed 11, tape kawashigi): the shipped agent is nearly optimal already, leaving +$1,353 on the table on melon and +$5,653 total across all six products — not the -$11,929 an earlier broken trickle model printed. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n- SUPERSEDED: `carrot_swap_2026-08-12.md`'s arms — two independent seed sets (1300-1303, 1306-1309), 384 games, `WHEAT_TILES_TO_CARROT=1` loses 10.4 and 7.8 win-rate points against 0 against every named opponent; `CARROT_SWAP` stayed False and no longer exists in the code (checked 2026-08-15); a count-based `CARROT_TILES=4`-from-day-20 was also a null (-1.1pp over 768 games) and was deleted. — `history/carrot_swap_2026-08-12.md`\n- `carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md` (120 seats replayed with `record_fills`): CARROT sold by 94.2% of seats (median 15 units), but WHEAT nets $33.83/tile-day season-long against CARROT's $29.79 (fertilized: $52.06 vs $42.59) — verdict is no general carrot increase, carrot only pays as an end-of-season closer on tiles too short for wheat/melon. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n- Carrot closing price correlates r=0.90 with the number of PET_CAFE/FARMERS_MARKET instances drawn (0 instances: $33-37 close; 3 instances: $56-69); TOMATO and EGG were sold by literally 0 of 120 leader seats, the genuinely empty books. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n- 87 recorded ladder tapes' closing inventory: MELON always finishes with a surplus (+38 to +158, no shop ever carries it, 29-unit/season appetite) and FERTILIZER always finishes with a surplus (+201 to +493, no shop, no drain) — every board over-plants both, winners included; WHEAT's closing surplus is negative in all 87 tapes (-386 to -1,356), carried by 5 of 8 shop types. — `history/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md`\n- Real plant counts (corrected from BUY_SEED order quantities, which re-post every turn): WHEAT 138, STRAWBERRY 36, MELON 19, CARROT 6, identical across five checked games — the earlier \"136 melon vs their 19\" claim was an artifact of counting orders, not plants. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- WHEAT vs CARROT per-cycle mechanics (`reference/kaggriculture.py`): WHEAT $10 seed/4-day cycle/8 plantings-season/28 unfertilized yield-per-tile vs CARROT $20 seed/3-day cycle/11 plantings-season/29 unfertilized yield-per-tile — CARROT needs 38% more cycles and costs 2x per planting for near-identical raw yield. — `history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`\n- Two 2026-08-13 ladder losses share one shape: `92550780` lost $69,881 to $77,275 while running 8 sheep against the opponent's 4 and shipping a fraction of the wool they did (28 units/$6,738 vs 115 units/$28,158); `92562037` lost $129,161 to $133,724 as a near-mirror where the one real divergence was melon (both sides plant 19 tiles once corrected). — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- `_daily_refresh_animals` removes an animal after two consecutive unfed days (`kaggriculture.py:804`); `92566708` lost 12 of 14 animals days 10-13 ($5,200 stock plus every remaining milk/wool unit for 17 days) while the shed held zero wheat days 8-12 and the bank sat at $1-$75-$22. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- CONTAMINATED: `LATE_SELL_BIAS`'s original measurement (pool win 14.6%->27.1%, money $16,146->$24,535, 2026-08-09) — `arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md` found `run_arena` drove one agent object through every match, and a fresh-agent-per-match re-run of the identical config gets $102,886/100% win against the contaminated $5,774/6.2%, a 17.8x gap; direction may still hold but the magnitude was never re-measured clean. — `history/fable_market_shape_2026-08-09.md`, `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n- The shipped artifact was never on the contaminated path: `scripts/gate_pool.py` rebuilds the agent per job and measured clean at 94.4% win / $108,180 mean against the 9-member COMPETITIVE pool, seeds 700-705, both seatings, 108 games. — `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n- Turning off every solved route from d12 costs ~4% on three-quadrant arms (straw_q3 -$5,906, melon_q3 -$7,539) while the mix barely moves (wheat tile count shifts by at most 3); the greedy alone runs a season in 8-25s against ~2 hours for the full solve, making it usable as a stage-1 mix ranker. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n- Our routing beats tetsuya's own on his own schedule with zero precomputed routes: $62,365 against his recorded $55,330 (+$7,035) — the gap to #1 is the crop/herd mix (stage 1), not execution. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n- Herd-split scoring fix (scored at his decisions, not final census) moved match rate 11.5% -> 55.8% -> 65.6% -> 77.9% purely by correcting the scoring method and the SHEEP-first opening floor (unanimous in 78/78 games), before any new rule; a milk-count threshold classifier (`MILK_SHOPS_FOR_A_BIG_HERD=2`) then pushed it to 88.5% (1051/1187), 21/21 two-milk-shop games going high-cow with zero false positives. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`, `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-20.md`\n- The prior \"opponent sheep count predicts our split\" lead was pseudo-replication (213 placements over ~50 games); re-measured at the game level the gap collapses to -0.05 to -0.32, noise. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-20.md`\n- TOMATO is gated at `TOMATO_DRAIN_SHOPS_NEEDED=2`: 0 of 34 games with fewer than two tomato-draining shops ever planted a tomato tile. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n- Route/mix coupling: a strawberry-cap change measured -$10,553 against stale routes and +$4,022 once re-solved for it; a melon arm with NO crop change at all showed +$5,615 purely from route staleness; 2 added geese lost -$62,866 against stale routes and -$63,417 re-solved (not rescued). — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n- Geese via `swap_in_geese` stayed broken even matched to geese-solved routes ($96,766 vs $97,317 cow-solved, both far below the $156,083-$160,183 no-geese baselines); geese from a schedule that natively contains coops (no swap) were harmless on 9/14 boards, going 6W-3L. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n- SUPERSEDED: `market_curves_and_the_q4_branch_2026-08-15.md`'s +$465-mean four-quadrant number — `q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`'s corrected measurement is -$716 mean, se $2,837, not distinguishable from zero on 11 seeds; the live-routing handicap alone (`shipped_live` vs `shipped`) costs -$1,873 and 2 wins, separable from the parcel/herd question. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`\n- Market-as-one-token (tetsuya, 78 games, 56,082 seat-turns): 80.7% of turns carry no market action; 346 distinct market bundles total, top 100 cover 98.5% — the case for one categorical market-bundle head instead of nine independent heads. — `history/the_market_is_one_token_not_nine_heads_2026-08-20.md`\n- Parity restored against kaggle-environments 1.32.6 (2026-08-09): four rules fixed (movement onto LOCKED tiles allowed, DROP/PICKUP/PLACE resolve before the LOCKED guard, BUY_* fails once shed is full, shops unlock WITH replacement capped at `MAX_SHOP_INSTANCES`); an all-distinct 8-shop draw has probability 0.24%, so duplicates are the norm on every parity seed tested. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n- The tape fixture `tests/test_tape_parity.py` had recorded `EXPECTED_BANK=$147,178` from `module_version 1.32.3`; replaying the identical tape into installed 1.32.6 banks $92,313 instead — the test now compares live against the installed env rather than a stale recording. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n- `run_arena` passed one agent OBJECT into every match instead of a factory (task #54, 2026-08-09): three consecutive matches through one object on seed 40 vs `pass` banked $114,028, then $0, then $0 — invalidation, not degradation; fix is `AgentFactory`, built once per match. — `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n- Pen-placement geometry is not a lever, confirmed a third time on two fresh loss episodes (`91229682`, `91231728`): our walk-per-work-op ratio is BETTER than the opponent's in both (1.028 vs 1.118; 1.210 vs 1.445) despite our pens sitting measurably farther from the shed (mean shed-dist 2.86 vs 1.21; 2.19 vs 1.57). — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- A pre-existing \"1.86 vs 2.93\" ring-depth figure does not reproduce under Manhattan (3.25/1.90) or Euclidean (2.69/1.50) centroid metrics tried at every 2-day checkpoint; same direction, different magnitude — cite 3.25/1.90 going forward. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- The shed-discard problem was overstated ~15x by counting `held_at_dusk - shed_at_dawn` as the discard: instrumenting `_drop_inventories_to_shed` directly gives 7 units lost on the canonical full season (against a $55,837 bank), not the 105-137 the delta method reported; the 123-unit figure is real only on the handover-11 fixture, which is not the scored surface. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n- `FEED_RESERVE_YIELDS_TO_OVERFLOW` had no day gate and fired from day 0 against a four-animal herd (day 14: OFF wheat=11/$63.0, ON wheat=21/$2,478.0); fixed by gating both guards to `day >= FEED_DAYS_FROM_DAY` (16), confirmed red again on revert. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n- Releasing the feed reserve to cut discards is NOT a clean win across 7 seed/mode combinations: discards got worse in 5 of 7, money worse in 4 of 7, including full-season seed 12 at -$13,304 for 39 fewer units discarded. — `history/fable_the_discard_problem_2026-08-09.md`\n- The wheat churn is a buy-side day-budget defect, not sell-side: the field buys 266-392 wheat units/season, we bought 1,099 (66-game average); `FEED_BUY_CEILING_DAYS=2` cuts season purchases 1,800->797 (-56%), keeps bank at parity (+$573 vs unfixed) and produces zero herd drops, matching the unfixed safety record. — `history/fable_the_wheat_churn_2026-08-09.md`\n- `FEED_BUY_CEILING_DAYS=1` (one day's true consumption) is a worse fix: it cuts purchases to 393 but starves the herd through a separate delivery bottleneck (seed 11 day 15: 16-19 idle wheat in the shed while only 7 of 20 animals get fed; herd drops 20->14 by day 17). — `history/fable_the_wheat_churn_2026-08-09.md`\n\n- The derived sell rule has one free game-theoretic dial, `contest`: `1.0` is the single-player optimum, `0.5` the two-player split, and lower values sell sooner; both `market` and `town` are `shared: True` in the observation, so the rule needs no opponent order visible to compute. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- First local peak of revenue by product (CONTEST=1.0): STRAWBERRY peaks at 224 units/$26,880 season revenue, MELON at 88 units/$15,748, WHEAT at 276 units/$6,900 — \"saturation\" must be read at the first local peak, since the $1 floor makes revenue rise again forever with volume; a first version of this table searched globally and wrongly called wheat/carrot/tomato/melon/egg/fertilizer all saturating at 3,000 units. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`\n- CORRECTED: geese are NOT an exception to saturation. An earlier claim that goose herds scale without limit came from `revenue_rate` computing herd output as `events * max_held` (max_held caps the unharvested buffer, not per-event yield, `kaggriculture.py:813`), overstating every herd 4-6x; corrected per-head daily output flattens at COW $1,164 (16+ head), SHEEP $897 (16+), GOOSE $246 (8+) — a cow herd's flat ceiling is 4.7x a goose herd's. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`\n- Fertilizer is species-independent with zero drain (`kaggriculture.py:818` sets `fertilizer_available=True` for every animal tile daily, no shop and no `TOWN_CENTER_PRODUCTS` entry carries it): marginal price runs $90/unit at 50 sold down to $1 at 500 sold, cumulative revenue plateaus at $25,052 — every one of 87 recorded ladder tapes closes with fertilizer between $1 and $20 of a $100 base. — `history/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md`, `history/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md`\n- Season absorption differs sharply between env versions: WHEAT drain 635 units (1.32.2) vs 525 (1.32.6), MELON 140 vs 30 — `kagg/search/chains.py`'s `DRAIN_UNITS` still held the 1.32.2 column as of 2026-08-09 and overstated the market's worth by 1.13x (carrot) to 4.67x (melon), $308,160 believed vs a real $207,180, though stage 1 had not yet shipped in `submission/main.py` at that point so no ladder game was cost by it. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Two-way design (6 tape opponents x 6 seeds, same agent/schedule/code, 36 seasons): final bank ranges from $16,888 to $163,036 with zero code changes; MILK realised price is board-driven (mean spread across seeds, opponent fixed: 88.5) and WOOL is opponent-driven (mean spread across opponents, seed fixed: 73.1) — WOOL is sold by 1 of 8 shops (thin demand, opponent volume dominates) while MILK is in 3 of 8 (the draw dominates). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- The \"high-value produce sits in hands at dusk, not the shed\" claim was false: `kagg/analysis/fills.py` on seeds 13/17/29/41 shows 100-109% of everything produced gets sold, 0 left in shed or hands at season end; the false reading came from comparing raw counts across different herd sizes and sampling at dusk (flow's max numerator, min denominator). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- A hand-turn census once reported \"idle 0.0%\" because the env's idle op is named `PASS`, not `IDLE`; the real split is 24% idle, 42% walking, ~34% working. — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- Shop-draw probability of never carrying a product in 8 draws with replacement: WHEAT 0.2%, STRAWBERRY 2.3%, MILK 2.3%, WOOL 34.4%, MELON always (no shop stocks it) — the numeric seed is hidden from observations but `town[\"unlocked_shops\"]` is `shared: True` and fully observable every turn. — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- The forum claim \"multiply base price by shop count\" is half right: shop count sets the sustainable drain *rate*, not the price — milk with three shops still floors after 76 units of excess, which is why our own milk repeatedly closed at $1-$5 despite the claim saying it \"sells above 160 most of the season.\" — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- Herd totals across 14 downloaded games cluster at (COW,SHEEP) = (6,8) in 9 games, (10,4) in 5; the four 13-animal (not 14) games are exactly the four games with a starved-animal escape, all from the same tile/day pattern: fed d8, missed d9 and d10, gone at d11 h00, a $400 replacement purchased the same day for an animal that never produced a unit. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- Melon front-load fix (`NO_DRAIN_THRESHOLD`, sell a no-drain product the day it arrives rather than trickling): test `tests/test_a_no_drain_product_is_sold_the_day_it_arrives.py` is red before the fix, green after, red again on revert; the race it fixes exists because recorded opponents dump 114-120 melons into the same shared book. — `history/fable_allocation_economics_2026-08-15.md`\n- The market-depth table computed three independent ways (base×shop-count arithmetic, units-to-$1-floor, and drain-with-price-at-N) agree on direction: WHEAT and EGG never floor; WOOL, MILK and STRAWBERRY are shallow (59-76 units to floor) and simultaneously the highest-value and easiest-to-destroy products. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`, `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`, `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- A price-neutral offer size does not track price level: 7 units of strawberry ($120 base) or 53 of fertilizer ($100 base) both hold the book flat — a seven-fold spread in tolerable offer size at similar base prices. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- \"Less, not slower\": across 56 real-loss cases, downscaling the sell (fewer units, same shape) gains +$254,293 total (best in 16 of 56); spreading (same units, even cadence) gains +$87,343 (best in 16); both together gain +$252,611 (best in 17) — cutting plus spreading adds nothing over cutting alone, except for WOOL, which is a pure volume problem (one episode: $5,278 observed vs $23,794 at 0.7x volume). — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Two traps caught twice: money is not the ladder objective (Seb's own sell mix earned more money on every seed and still lost 8/8 head-to-head, because ceding wool let the opponent earn more than we gained); and a replayed tape does not react to being beaten, so absolute money against a tape runs 2-3x a real ladder game. — `history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`\n- Shop-to-product map (units/day per instance): WHEAT is carried by 5 shop types (bakery/pizza/brunch/ice-cream/farmers, 6 each), STRAWBERRY by 4, MILK by 3, CARROT/TOMATO/EGG by 2 each, WOOL by 1 (yarn store), MELON by none — shops unlock one per three days, drawn with replacement, capped at `MAX_SHOP_INSTANCES=8`. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n- The shipped plan's season sell totals against daily drain: FERTILIZER (1,487 sells against 0 drain/day) and MELON (120 sells against 1/day, 4x the season's absorption) are the two lines that break the budget; CARROT, MILK, STRAWBERRY and WOOL absorb within 1.1-14.7 days. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n- Melon/strawberry tile-swap payoff is CONDITIONAL, not universal: one 2026-08-13 loss (`92562037`, 5 strawberry-carrying shop instances, drained 895, closed $187 above base) says a melon tile converted to strawberry pays several times more; another same-day loss (`92550780`, 1 instance, drained 179, closed $3, floored) says the swap buys nothing because that town wanted wool and carrot instead — a global sweep over `melon_day`/`melon_shift` averages both regimes and reports a wash, the expected result of asking a global question about a conditional quantity. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- EGG closed above base in all five games checked directly ($59-$88, town draining up to 1,074 units) with zero geese run in any of the five — EGG was unsellable at all when the original goose flag was measured (see `animals_and_the_herd.md`). — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n- `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` correctly held cows at cap 6 on a three-YARN_STORE board (`92550780`) but `_herd_split`'s early return is forbidden from growing `want_total` beyond the schedule's own count, so the rule's own reference table (COW 6, SHEEP 12, total 18) landed as COW 6, SHEEP 8, total 14 — four animals short, because the early return skips the drain-aware greedy branch below it. — `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n\n## Open\n\n- Whether the shop draw is seed-only or agent-dependent: the weed-spawn RNG draws before the shop unlock and depends on which tiles are empty, so agent play shifts the shop sequence (seed 13/29 examples where PASS-vs-PASS and real play diverge). — `history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`\n- Whether the q4 four-quadrant branch generalises off its 15.7%-of-seeds early-yarn trigger population — not tested outside seeds 0-69 and a frozen mirror opponent. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`\n- Carrot has no predictor found yet at the per-game level: 9 games at drain counts 1-3 are evenly spread and non-monotone in whether carrot gets planted. — `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-19.md`\n- Whether the fast-sim 300/300 parity gate has been re-run since the 2026-08-15 1.32.7 hinge fix landed in `kagg/sim/market.py` and `kagg/sim/jax_sim.py` — no doc names a fresh full-episode run since, while `CLAUDE.md` states parity PASSES as fact with no version qualifier. — `history/parity_restored_on_1326_2026-08-09.md`\n- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT=True` (always-nearest pen siting) was reverted for being penalised by a cell-count-resemblance-to-tape proxy metric, not a bank/win-rate test; its own docstring calls this unresolved and no bank/win-rate re-test has been run. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- A wheat 2-4-unit close-of-season residue in the shed is unexplained; the regression test was relaxed to `<= 3` rather than the defect being fixed. — `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n\n## Contradictions\n\n- Melon-swap rule direction: root `RETRACTIONS.md` item 7 (960-game population sweep, outside `docs/`) has the shipped rule (`MELON_YIELD_OPPONENT_MELON=7`) narrowly BEATING it off (`=99`), 68.1% vs 67.9% win. `when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`'s model and `work_to_be_done_2026-08-16.md` §4's direct A/B (-$17,297/game, 66.7% vs 0%) both say keeping melon beats swapping. Not resolved. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`, `history/work_to_be_done_2026-08-16.md`\n- `LATE_SELL_BIAS`'s original magnitude (14.6%->27.1% win, $16,146->$24,535) vs the state-leak-corrected re-run of the identical config (6.2%->100% win, $5,774->$102,886) — a 17.8x gap on money and win rate alike; neither file reconciles the two. — `history/fable_market_shape_2026-08-09.md`, `history/arena_state_leak_2026-08-09.md`\n- Four-quadrant herd size: `q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`'s brief and code target COW 6/SHEEP 12=18 for the 2026-08-14 100-tile field; the same-day `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` measurement over 74 Kawashigi seats finds four-quadrant herds run 13-16, never 18. Both are stated of different players; not reconciled into one target number. — `history/q4_variant_first_draft_2026-08-15.md`, `history/the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`\n- STALE: `when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`'s headline total (+$12,136 from spreading sales evenly) compares against an infeasible baseline; the real release value is +$5,653 (see the melon release line above), and its own falsifiable prediction (`MELON_YIELD_OPPONENT_MELON=99` should beat `=7`) was tested on 960 games and falsified. — `history/when_and_what_to_sell_2026-08-15.md`\n","numLines":96,"startLine":1,"totalLines":96}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"14771b35-09cb-4abe-9bd7-03b866c908b6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}
