{"parentUuid":"47173724-bdf4-41e5-8678-f1503ea8ba80","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"74a006da-e2d3-4f09-8619-d25d7592b2b7","agentId":"a76e037eedc47105d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JMJMu4F8XbxkmVz5tDWbS4","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Animals and the herd\n2\t\n3\tSettled facts on the herd (cow/sheep/goose) mix question, the geese verdict, and pen-placement\n4\tgeometry. Read `docs/geese_verdict_status_2026-08-22.md` for the current, live status of the geese\n5\tquestion — it postdates every file merged here and is not touched by this pass.\n6\tThe geese question has been re-derived at least six times in this project; two more derivations\n7\tlive in `docs/market_and_economics.md` (`the_market_is_the_late_season_2026-08-13.md` and\n8\t`the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`).\n9\tFull detail for every line below is in `docs/history/`, named per line.\n10\t\n11\t## Settled\n12\t\n13\t- Before 2026-08-13, `SELLABLE` listed only six of nine products — CARROT, TOMATO and EGG could not be sold at all — so every geese/eggs sweep before commit `c99a8a5` measured pure cost against zero revenue, on any flag, on any day; not evidence about geese. This is the third time in this project a mechanic-level negative turned out to be an artifact of our own code. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n14\t- RETRACTED within its own file: an early money-only geese comparison used opponent volumes summed from sell OFFERS, not fills (agents re-post an unfilled quota every turn, inflating the true number ~6x); re-measured against market inventory, two full opposing herds move ~60-70 units of wool a season (not 500) and milk realises $37-$78/unit on a milk-poor board, $231 on a milk-rich one. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n15\t- On the (retracted) offer-based volumes, GOOSE nets $11,270 with 2 shops draining vs $9,818 with 0 (an 11% loss when its drain disappears); COW nets $10,392 vs $556 (77% loss); SHEEP nets $32,910 vs $1,174 (88% loss) — the ASYMMETRY (goose's floor stays close to its mean because EGG's price curve is undentable log/0.2) survives the retraction even though the absolute numbers do not. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n16\t- SUPERSEDED by the Elo/margin verdict below: on this file's own expectation table, a cow or sheep beats a goose by about $8,000 per six animals on MONEY — \"the math does NOT say switch to geese,\" but the goose's worst-decile revenue is more than double either alternative's, because EGG's book is uncontested by construction (opponents run fixed scripts that cannot start producing eggs in response). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n17\t- The closing geese verdict, on the objective that pays (Elo margin, not money): commissioned as an audit of the money verdict above, math only, same shared book and opponent volumes but pro-rata drain share. Corrects a real -2-egg production error (44 sellable, not 46) and finds cow/sheep revenue in the money verdict was optimistic (COW $78/unit not $118, SHEEP $88/unit not $125) — ordering (GOOSE < COW < SHEEP on money) survives either way. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n18\t- Suppression-value modelling over 4,000 draws: goose margin expectation is $11,758 WORSE than cow/sheep per six animals, because our marginal milk/wool units force pro-rata floor-sharing onto the opponent's LARGER flow (1.7x our volume) while eggs are uncontested and safe-and-harmless — \"safe-and-harmless is exactly what a zero-sum ladder does not pay for.\" Goose is margin-best in only 0.9% of 4,000 draws (COW 43.6%, SHEEP 55.5%), rising to only 7.0% at its single best observable corner (no YARN_STORE, no milk carrier among the first three shops by d9, 12.8% of games) — no observable rescues it. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n19\t- Policy stated by the closing audit: zero geese (-$11,758 margin expectation per six animals); `SHOP_ADAPT_HERD` (the cow<->sheep float) stays, near-optimal at COW 43.6%/SHEEP 55.5% margin-best split; the `HERD_GEESE` deletion (commit `7a88d30`) was right, but by accident — the sweep it rested on measured unsellable eggs (the `SELLABLE` bug), and the corrected arithmetic reaches the same end state by a different, now-verified route. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n20\t- Real ladder losses (read off replays only, not simulation; \"Alex downloads losses\" caveat applies — nothing here is a win rate): all three games ran herd COW 9-10 / SHEEP 4, and MILK floored in all three ($3, $5, $1) — \"that is not a seed, it is 9-10 cows.\" — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n21\t- Solver seating does not predict the result: the one WIN of three replays had the WORST route seating (58% of stops seated) while both losses seated 67% — this closes the \"regenerated routes generalise off seed 11\" question partly: 100% on the solving seed against 58-67% on a ladder board IS the measured overfit. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n22\t- Melon sells late because of delivery, not the sell gate: on d10, melon sits in HANDS (not the shed) for 7 hours while its book price holds at $272 — realised at ~$218/unit average when selling from h12 instead would have averaged ~$250, a real ~$1,900 prize but not the reason either game was lost. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n23\t- A cow starved on d11 and a $400 replacement was purchased for an animal already owned: `_daily_refresh_animals` removes an animal after two consecutive unfed days; traced turn-by-turn, one cow was fed d8, missed d9 and d10, removed at d11 h00, and a replacement placed d11 h7 — it starved before producing a single unit of milk. Same tile, same day, in 3 of 4 escape cases across 18 downloaded games; FEED has the highest measured op-to-money correlation in this project (+0.452). — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n24\t- The d9/d10 stranded-FEED defect above is CLOSED in live play: two later downloaded losses show zero animal starvations on our side in either game (opponent lost 1 sheep in each, both d29 h00) — treat the two losses as independent, not pooled. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n25\t- Sheep/wool problem confirmed again but did not decide either of those two later games: WOOL closed at the $1.00 floor in both (zero YARN_STORE unlocked on either board, we ran 4 sheep anyway), but BOTH seats made the same mistake in one game (one ran 5 sheep to our 4, into the same dead book) so it is not the differentiator there. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n26\t- Melon in those two games: we sell faster (clearing our whole stock at the two best prices of the season) but simply grow less of it — 12 melon fills against the opponent's 62 in one game, roughly $3,700 of the $6,892 total loss margin at their realised prices. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n27\t- Killed, do not re-derive: \"we dump sales in one hour and they spread theirs\" — we post SELL orders in 68 hours across the season against the opponent's 319, but priced on a real d29 order, spreading 35 WHEAT over 12 hours instead of 1 gains only +$3 (MILK +$42, STRAWBERRY +$19, CARROT/WOOL/FERTILIZER +$0) — `_process_market` quotes per unit at live inventory, so the walk-down is identical whether posted in 1 hour or 12. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n28\t- Leading on bank+field value at d28 does not guarantee the win: one game led by +$5,311 at d28, decayed to +$2,631 at dawn d29, and finished -$962 — a comparison game showed the identical shape (+$4,503 -> +$872 -> -$1,562). Neither is a d29 routing miss (both boards finish with negligible standing stock); the opponent simply had more to harvest on the final day (25-38 HARVEST ops vs our fewer). — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n29\t- Pen-placement geometry confirmed NOT a lever, a third time, on two fresh loss episodes nobody had analysed before (91229682, 91231728): our walk-per-work-op ratio is BETTER than the opponent's in both episodes (1.028 vs 1.118; 1.210 vs 1.445) despite our pens sitting measurably farther from the shed — the shape difference (smeared vs compact) is real but the assumed walking cost is not there. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n30\t- Correction to a pre-existing \"1.86 vs 2.93\" ring-depth figure: it does not reproduce under Manhattan (3.25/1.90) or Euclidean (2.69/1.50) centroid-distance metrics tried at every 2-day checkpoint from day 0-30 — same direction (we are more spread out), different magnitude; cite 3.25/1.90 going forward. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n31\t- What actually separates the two loss episodes analysed for geometry is production volume and idle time, not pen location: PASS 1512 vs 1005 and 1746 vs 354; WATER 578 vs 930 and 590 vs 1133; PLANT 81 vs 174 and 81 vs 163 — we lose both games regardless of the geometry finding. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n32\t- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT = True` (always-place-nearest) was tried and reverted: it achieves no pen beyond 5 steps at day 11, but was refused by the project's cell-count-resemblance-to-tape proxy metric at the time, not by a bank/win-rate test — its own docstring flags this as unresolved and asks for a bank/win-rate re-test, which has never been done. `router.DEPOT_RING_FIRST` (the field-order-level sibling) ships off because it reversed sign between tuning seeds (+$20,526) and held-out seeds (-$11,111), a winner's-curse null, not a proven loss. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n33\t\n34\t- Production model per animal, stepped through `_daily_refresh_animals` (`reference/kaggriculture.py:805`): GOOSE costs $300, COOP structure, first yield day 4, interval 1, max held 4, product EGG; COW costs $400, PASTURE, first yield day 8, interval 2, max held 6, product MILK; SHEEP costs $500, PASTURE, first yield day 6, interval 3, max held 6, product WOOL — the goose is the cheapest, starts earliest, and produces every day against the cow's every-other and sheep's every-third. Validated: 30 milk/cow predicted, a real game with 9 cows produced ~290 milk (32/cow). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n35\t- Expectation over the real shop draw (shops unlock 1-per-3-days on d3-d24, 8 draws with replacement over 8 types, P(carrier)=Binomial(8,m/8) for m carrier types): GOOSE/EGG has 2 carrier types (P(zero carriers) 10.0%), COW/MILK has 3 (2.3%), SHEEP/WOOL has 1 (34.4%) — the goose's worst-decile revenue is the closest of the three to its mean because its book is hardest to leave empty AND hardest to crash (EGG's price curve is log, needing ~2 million units above I0 to halve). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n36\t- Costs left out of the original money verdict, priced in the closing audit: feed cost cancels across species (~$3.2k-$6.5k for 6 animals, charged to \"having animals at all,\" not to species choice); the goose's extra harvest-visit op cost (12 visits against cow's 6, sheep's 7) is bounded under $300 even at a panic $50/turn; the goose actually SAVES ops elsewhere because its undentable book makes release-timing worthless (no h05-style shed race, unlike milk). — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n37\t- Caveats stated in the closing audit: opponent volumes are three replays, but the SIGN of the verdict is robust to +/-50% on them; EGG=0 for the opponent is structural (fixed scripts cannot respond), not a volume estimate; fill-sharing is modelled pro-rata within a day (a simplification of the env's real per-order-slot interleave, applied symmetrically to both sides of every comparison); only 6-of-one-species portfolios are compared, mixed herds are not measured and nothing here rewards mixing geese in. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n38\t- 93239838 (a 2026-08-15 loss): town wanted eggs and strawberry, we made milk and wool — WHEAT drained 1,253 units and still closed starved at $49 (base $25); EGG drained 1,074 units with nobody producing any, closing $154 (base $50); MILK drained only 179 units and still floored at $3 (base $160); WOOL drained 0 units (no YARN_STORE all season) and floored at $1 (base $200) — whole-season wool on that game was ~130 units for $1,940 against 4 sheep costing $500 each plus a season of FEED/CARE. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n39\t- 93242536 (a different 2026-08-15 loss, different shop draw, same failure on a different product): CARROT drained 895 units at $77 (base $35, \"starved,\" only 63 tiles planted); WOOL was fine on this board (YARN_STORE drawn twice, closed $236); MILK floored anyway at $1 despite 537 units drained — the failure follows the shop draw, not a fixed crop weakness. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n40\t- `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` correctly held cows at cap 6 on a three-YARN_STORE board, but `_herd_split`'s early return is forbidden from growing `want_total` beyond the schedule's own count — so the rule's own reference table (COW 6, SHEEP 12, total 18) landed as COW 6, SHEEP 8, total 14, four animals short, because the early return skips the drain-aware greedy branch below it. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n41\t- TOMATO closed at 11x base ($660 vs $60) in one of the two later-analysed losses, drained 895 units, with neither seat growing it — on the steep `hinge` below-curve added in 1.32.7 (inventory 10,000->$60, 9,800->$84, 9,600->$300, 9,400->$900, 9,200->$1,884); caveat stated: the curve is this steep in both directions, so oversupplying it would collapse it fast too, and sizing a conditional tomato play was not established in this file. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n42\t- Pen-siting mechanism traced to source (`_refresh_pins`, `schedule_agent.py` ~1672-1710): falls through `PEN_SPOTS` (a fixed priority list, currently `()`, empty and shipped off — its own docstring records a 2-cell-out-of-150 measured difference from filling it, not worth carrying) to shed-access tiles if free, to nearest-depot search only if `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT` is True (it is False) or day >= `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT_FROM_DAY` (11), otherwise falling through to the same quadrant-major boustrophedon sweep crops use — which is why an early-requested pen lands at the tail of the sweep. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n43\t- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT_FROM_DAY = 11` ships because early-game distance already matches the tape exactly (22 vs 22 through day 8), and the benefit of switching earlier is small given the same file's own finding that walking is not the binding cost. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n44\t\n45\t## Open\n46\t\n47\t- Whether `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT = True` would actually win on a bank/win-rate test rather than the proxy metric that refused it — flagged as worth re-testing, but the geometry section's own finding (walking isn't the binding cost even under the current worse layout) makes this low-expected-value. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n48\t- Whether the melon-timing prize identified in the ladder-loss replays (~$1,900 from a mid-day DROP at the shed) has been built — identified as open work, not implemented in this set. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n49\t- Whether `SHOP_ADAPT_HERD`'s next candidate lever — the herd COUNT rather than the cow/sheep SPLIT it already floats — pays off; flagged as needing the same A/B discipline the melon rule got, not measured here. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n50\t\n51\t## Contradictions\n52\t\n53\tNone found in this set.\n54\t"}]},"uuid":"37703942-9658-4b6b-840a-f1a18810daf3","timestamp":"2026-08-24T11:38:30.404Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/docs/animals_and_the_herd.md","content":"# Animals and the herd\n\nSettled facts on the herd (cow/sheep/goose) mix question, the geese verdict, and pen-placement\ngeometry. Read `docs/geese_verdict_status_2026-08-22.md` for the current, live status of the geese\nquestion — it postdates every file merged here and is not touched by this pass.\nThe geese question has been re-derived at least six times in this project; two more derivations\nlive in `docs/market_and_economics.md` (`the_market_is_the_late_season_2026-08-13.md` and\n`the_mix_and_the_routes_are_one_artifact_2026-08-19.md`).\nFull detail for every line below is in `docs/history/`, named per line.\n\n## Settled\n\n- Before 2026-08-13, `SELLABLE` listed only six of nine products — CARROT, TOMATO and EGG could not be sold at all — so every geese/eggs sweep before commit `c99a8a5` measured pure cost against zero revenue, on any flag, on any day; not evidence about geese. This is the third time in this project a mechanic-level negative turned out to be an artifact of our own code. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- RETRACTED within its own file: an early money-only geese comparison used opponent volumes summed from sell OFFERS, not fills (agents re-post an unfilled quota every turn, inflating the true number ~6x); re-measured against market inventory, two full opposing herds move ~60-70 units of wool a season (not 500) and milk realises $37-$78/unit on a milk-poor board, $231 on a milk-rich one. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- On the (retracted) offer-based volumes, GOOSE nets $11,270 with 2 shops draining vs $9,818 with 0 (an 11% loss when its drain disappears); COW nets $10,392 vs $556 (77% loss); SHEEP nets $32,910 vs $1,174 (88% loss) — the ASYMMETRY (goose's floor stays close to its mean because EGG's price curve is undentable log/0.2) survives the retraction even though the absolute numbers do not. — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- SUPERSEDED by the Elo/margin verdict below: on this file's own expectation table, a cow or sheep beats a goose by about $8,000 per six animals on MONEY — \"the math does NOT say switch to geese,\" but the goose's worst-decile revenue is more than double either alternative's, because EGG's book is uncontested by construction (opponents run fixed scripts that cannot start producing eggs in response). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- The closing geese verdict, on the objective that pays (Elo margin, not money): commissioned as an audit of the money verdict above, math only, same shared book and opponent volumes but pro-rata drain share. Corrects a real -2-egg production error (44 sellable, not 46) and finds cow/sheep revenue in the money verdict was optimistic (COW $78/unit not $118, SHEEP $88/unit not $125) — ordering (GOOSE < COW < SHEEP on money) survives either way. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n- Suppression-value modelling over 4,000 draws: goose margin expectation is $11,758 WORSE than cow/sheep per six animals, because our marginal milk/wool units force pro-rata floor-sharing onto the opponent's LARGER flow (1.7x our volume) while eggs are uncontested and safe-and-harmless — \"safe-and-harmless is exactly what a zero-sum ladder does not pay for.\" Goose is margin-best in only 0.9% of 4,000 draws (COW 43.6%, SHEEP 55.5%), rising to only 7.0% at its single best observable corner (no YARN_STORE, no milk carrier among the first three shops by d9, 12.8% of games) — no observable rescues it. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n- Policy stated by the closing audit: zero geese (-$11,758 margin expectation per six animals); `SHOP_ADAPT_HERD` (the cow<->sheep float) stays, near-optimal at COW 43.6%/SHEEP 55.5% margin-best split; the `HERD_GEESE` deletion (commit `7a88d30`) was right, but by accident — the sweep it rested on measured unsellable eggs (the `SELLABLE` bug), and the corrected arithmetic reaches the same end state by a different, now-verified route. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n- Real ladder losses (read off replays only, not simulation; \"Alex downloads losses\" caveat applies — nothing here is a win rate): all three games ran herd COW 9-10 / SHEEP 4, and MILK floored in all three ($3, $5, $1) — \"that is not a seed, it is 9-10 cows.\" — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- Solver seating does not predict the result: the one WIN of three replays had the WORST route seating (58% of stops seated) while both losses seated 67% — this closes the \"regenerated routes generalise off seed 11\" question partly: 100% on the solving seed against 58-67% on a ladder board IS the measured overfit. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- Melon sells late because of delivery, not the sell gate: on d10, melon sits in HANDS (not the shed) for 7 hours while its book price holds at $272 — realised at ~$218/unit average when selling from h12 instead would have averaged ~$250, a real ~$1,900 prize but not the reason either game was lost. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- A cow starved on d11 and a $400 replacement was purchased for an animal already owned: `_daily_refresh_animals` removes an animal after two consecutive unfed days; traced turn-by-turn, one cow was fed d8, missed d9 and d10, removed at d11 h00, and a replacement placed d11 h7 — it starved before producing a single unit of milk. Same tile, same day, in 3 of 4 escape cases across 18 downloaded games; FEED has the highest measured op-to-money correlation in this project (+0.452). — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- The d9/d10 stranded-FEED defect above is CLOSED in live play: two later downloaded losses show zero animal starvations on our side in either game (opponent lost 1 sheep in each, both d29 h00) — treat the two losses as independent, not pooled. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Sheep/wool problem confirmed again but did not decide either of those two later games: WOOL closed at the $1.00 floor in both (zero YARN_STORE unlocked on either board, we ran 4 sheep anyway), but BOTH seats made the same mistake in one game (one ran 5 sheep to our 4, into the same dead book) so it is not the differentiator there. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Melon in those two games: we sell faster (clearing our whole stock at the two best prices of the season) but simply grow less of it — 12 melon fills against the opponent's 62 in one game, roughly $3,700 of the $6,892 total loss margin at their realised prices. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Killed, do not re-derive: \"we dump sales in one hour and they spread theirs\" — we post SELL orders in 68 hours across the season against the opponent's 319, but priced on a real d29 order, spreading 35 WHEAT over 12 hours instead of 1 gains only +$3 (MILK +$42, STRAWBERRY +$19, CARROT/WOOL/FERTILIZER +$0) — `_process_market` quotes per unit at live inventory, so the walk-down is identical whether posted in 1 hour or 12. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Leading on bank+field value at d28 does not guarantee the win: one game led by +$5,311 at d28, decayed to +$2,631 at dawn d29, and finished -$962 — a comparison game showed the identical shape (+$4,503 -> +$872 -> -$1,562). Neither is a d29 routing miss (both boards finish with negligible standing stock); the opponent simply had more to harvest on the final day (25-38 HARVEST ops vs our fewer). — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Pen-placement geometry confirmed NOT a lever, a third time, on two fresh loss episodes nobody had analysed before (91229682, 91231728): our walk-per-work-op ratio is BETTER than the opponent's in both episodes (1.028 vs 1.118; 1.210 vs 1.445) despite our pens sitting measurably farther from the shed — the shape difference (smeared vs compact) is real but the assumed walking cost is not there. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- Correction to a pre-existing \"1.86 vs 2.93\" ring-depth figure: it does not reproduce under Manhattan (3.25/1.90) or Euclidean (2.69/1.50) centroid-distance metrics tried at every 2-day checkpoint from day 0-30 — same direction (we are more spread out), different magnitude; cite 3.25/1.90 going forward. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- What actually separates the two loss episodes analysed for geometry is production volume and idle time, not pen location: PASS 1512 vs 1005 and 1746 vs 354; WATER 578 vs 930 and 590 vs 1133; PLANT 81 vs 174 and 81 vs 163 — we lose both games regardless of the geometry finding. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT = True` (always-place-nearest) was tried and reverted: it achieves no pen beyond 5 steps at day 11, but was refused by the project's cell-count-resemblance-to-tape proxy metric at the time, not by a bank/win-rate test — its own docstring flags this as unresolved and asks for a bank/win-rate re-test, which has never been done. `router.DEPOT_RING_FIRST` (the field-order-level sibling) ships off because it reversed sign between tuning seeds (+$20,526) and held-out seeds (-$11,111), a winner's-curse null, not a proven loss. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n\n- Production model per animal, stepped through `_daily_refresh_animals` (`reference/kaggriculture.py:805`): GOOSE costs $300, COOP structure, first yield day 4, interval 1, max held 4, product EGG; COW costs $400, PASTURE, first yield day 8, interval 2, max held 6, product MILK; SHEEP costs $500, PASTURE, first yield day 6, interval 3, max held 6, product WOOL — the goose is the cheapest, starts earliest, and produces every day against the cow's every-other and sheep's every-third. Validated: 30 milk/cow predicted, a real game with 9 cows produced ~290 milk (32/cow). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- Expectation over the real shop draw (shops unlock 1-per-3-days on d3-d24, 8 draws with replacement over 8 types, P(carrier)=Binomial(8,m/8) for m carrier types): GOOSE/EGG has 2 carrier types (P(zero carriers) 10.0%), COW/MILK has 3 (2.3%), SHEEP/WOOL has 1 (34.4%) — the goose's worst-decile revenue is the closest of the three to its mean because its book is hardest to leave empty AND hardest to crash (EGG's price curve is log, needing ~2 million units above I0 to halve). — `history/the_goose_is_insurance_2026-08-15.md`\n- Costs left out of the original money verdict, priced in the closing audit: feed cost cancels across species (~$3.2k-$6.5k for 6 animals, charged to \"having animals at all,\" not to species choice); the goose's extra harvest-visit op cost (12 visits against cow's 6, sheep's 7) is bounded under $300 even at a panic $50/turn; the goose actually SAVES ops elsewhere because its undentable book makes release-timing worthless (no h05-style shed race, unlike milk). — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n- Caveats stated in the closing audit: opponent volumes are three replays, but the SIGN of the verdict is robust to +/-50% on them; EGG=0 for the opponent is structural (fixed scripts cannot respond), not a volume estimate; fill-sharing is modelled pro-rata within a day (a simplification of the env's real per-order-slot interleave, applied symmetrically to both sides of every comparison); only 6-of-one-species portfolios are compared, mixed herds are not measured and nothing here rewards mixing geese in. — `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`\n- 93239838 (a 2026-08-15 loss): town wanted eggs and strawberry, we made milk and wool — WHEAT drained 1,253 units and still closed starved at $49 (base $25); EGG drained 1,074 units with nobody producing any, closing $154 (base $50); MILK drained only 179 units and still floored at $3 (base $160); WOOL drained 0 units (no YARN_STORE all season) and floored at $1 (base $200) — whole-season wool on that game was ~130 units for $1,940 against 4 sheep costing $500 each plus a season of FEED/CARE. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- 93242536 (a different 2026-08-15 loss, different shop draw, same failure on a different product): CARROT drained 895 units at $77 (base $35, \"starved,\" only 63 tiles planted); WOOL was fine on this board (YARN_STORE drawn twice, closed $236); MILK floored anyway at $1 despite 537 units drained — the failure follows the shop draw, not a fixed crop weakness. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` correctly held cows at cap 6 on a three-YARN_STORE board, but `_herd_split`'s early return is forbidden from growing `want_total` beyond the schedule's own count — so the rule's own reference table (COW 6, SHEEP 12, total 18) landed as COW 6, SHEEP 8, total 14, four animals short, because the early return skips the drain-aware greedy branch below it. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- TOMATO closed at 11x base ($660 vs $60) in one of the two later-analysed losses, drained 895 units, with neither seat growing it — on the steep `hinge` below-curve added in 1.32.7 (inventory 10,000->$60, 9,800->$84, 9,600->$300, 9,400->$900, 9,200->$1,884); caveat stated: the curve is this steep in both directions, so oversupplying it would collapse it fast too, and sizing a conditional tomato play was not established in this file. — `history/two_losses_2026-08-15b.md`\n- Pen-siting mechanism traced to source (`_refresh_pins`, `schedule_agent.py` ~1672-1710): falls through `PEN_SPOTS` (a fixed priority list, currently `()`, empty and shipped off — its own docstring records a 2-cell-out-of-150 measured difference from filling it, not worth carrying) to shed-access tiles if free, to nearest-depot search only if `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT` is True (it is False) or day >= `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT_FROM_DAY` (11), otherwise falling through to the same quadrant-major boustrophedon sweep crops use — which is why an early-requested pen lands at the tail of the sweep. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT_FROM_DAY = 11` ships because early-game distance already matches the tape exactly (22 vs 22 through day 8), and the benefit of switching earlier is small given the same file's own finding that walking is not the binding cost. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n\n## Open\n\n- Whether `PEN_NEAREST_DEPOT = True` would actually win on a bank/win-rate test rather than the proxy metric that refused it — flagged as worth re-testing, but the geometry section's own finding (walking isn't the binding cost even under the current worse layout) makes this low-expected-value. — `history/geometry_of_the_herd_2026-08-09.md`\n- Whether the melon-timing prize identified in the ladder-loss replays (~$1,900 from a mid-day DROP at the shed) has been built — identified as open work, not implemented in this set. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n- Whether `SHOP_ADAPT_HERD`'s next candidate lever — the herd COUNT rather than the cow/sheep SPLIT it already floats — pays off; flagged as needing the same A/B discipline the melon rule got, not measured here. — `history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`\n\n## Contradictions\n\nNone found in this set.\n","numLines":54,"startLine":1,"totalLines":54}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"47173724-bdf4-41e5-8678-f1503ea8ba80","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

+0.166\n\nThe confound is real but small. Controlling for it:\n\n    win%              ours-low          ours-high\n    carrot-low     59.1% (n=252)     60.6% (n=315)\n    carrot-high    49.0% (n=294)     45.6% (n=147)\n\n- Across the rows: demand for **our** crops moves us **+1.5 points**. Nothing.\n- Down the columns: carrot demand costs **-10.1 points** when the town wants little of ours, and\n  **-15.0 points** when it wants a lot.\n\nCarrot dominates, and the effect is *stronger* in towns that also want our crops — the opposite of\nwhat a substitution artifact would do.\n\n## What this adds to the settled rule\n\nThe conditional rule's strongest bucket is `town CA>=4`, and it rests on 3 towns / 57 seasons.\nThe drain table above is 48 towns / 9 opponents / 1,008 seasons and points the same way: the towns\nwhere we lose are the carrot-hungry ones, and it is not a substitution artifact.\n`town CA>=4` and `drain_CARROT >= 5` are the same population seen from two directions, so the two\nmeasurements agree on a far wider base than either alone.\n\nWhat it does **not** settle, and what the killed sweeps were for:\n\n    carrot2_d25d27      two tiles in the window\n    carrot4_d25d27      four tiles in the window        <- how many, at the known-good days\n    x_carrot            the static swap\n    cond_carrot_d4_o7   the runtime rule at town CA>=4, opp CA<=7   <- gate width\n\nNothing new needs designing. Re-run them, six seeds, and read the arms against each other rather\nthan against the question of whether carrot works.\n\nSecond, separate item: **`SHOP_ADAPT_CROPS` (C-23) is still `False` on a void number.**\nIts `WHEAT<->CARROT` verdict of 0.806 against HEAD's 0.871 was measured on 2026-08-11, two days\nbefore `c99a8a5` connected carrot's revenue line.\n`CLAUDE.md` records that a decision to skip a fix can expire when the problem changes.\nThis one has, and the register already says UNVERIFIED.\n\n## Caveats\n\n- Win rate against the seated opponent, not Elo.\n- 3 seeds; project seed variance is ~15%. A 15-point split on 819 seasons is outside that; the\n  drain-6 row (57.8%, n=147) breaking the monotone trend is not explained.\n- `drain_*` is town demand, not our fills. It says what the town would buy, not what we sold it.\n","numLines":138,"startLine":1,"totalLines":138}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a36c86a1-0139-43b5-a838-7b52e9fbc661","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

This was misread as a day\n20\tduring analysis and corrected.\n21\t\n22\t## Result\n23\t\n24\t48 towns x 7 opponents x 3 seeds = 1008 paired seasons per arm. Pairing is exact: every arm sees\n25\tthe same (town, tape, opponent, seed).\n26\t\n27\t    arm                     mean ours     win%    vs ctrl   rows differing\n28\t    control                     88282     54.7         +0        0/1008\n29\t    carrot2_d25d27              88316     55.3        +34     1006/1008\n30\t    carrot4_d25d27              88539     56.2       +257     1008/1008\n31\t    cond_carrot_d3_o3           89377     60.2      +1096      630/1008\n32\t    cond_carrot_d3_o7           89377     60.2      +1096      630/1008\n33\t    cond_carrot_d3_o99          89377     60.2  +1080      441/1008\n39\t    cond_carrot_d5_o99          89361     60.0      +1080      441/1008\n40\t\n41\tWin rate by opponent band:\n42\t\n43\t    arm                  low    mid    top cond_carrot_d4_o*   54.9   54.2   63.0    68.8\n48\t    cond_carrot_d5_o*   54.9   54.2   63.0    68.1\n49\t\n50\tPaired McNemar against control, on the win/loss flip:\n51\t\n52\t    cond_carrot_d4_o3 real\n57\t\n58\t**The town condition works and is the load-bearing half.** `drain_min` moves the arm monotonically\n59\t-- 630 / 525 / 441 rows touched as the threshold rises 3 -> 4 -> 5 -- and the win rate lifts\n60\t54.7% -> 60.1% overall, including 58.6% -> 63.0% against the top band. 55 games flip to a win and\n61\tnone flips to a loss. This is a win-rate result, which is the selection criterion; the +$1,130 of\n62\tmoney is inside seed variance and should not be quoted as the finding.\n63\t\n64\tThe static `carrot4_d25d27` is much weaker (z=2.53, and it loses 12 games it used to win). Making\n65\tthe decision at runtime, where it can see the board, is what the conditional buys.\n66\t\n67\t## Where the win actually lives -- the aggregate was hiding it\n68\t\n69\tThe 60.1% headline is an average over towns the rule never touches. The recommended `drain_min=4` buys one bucket worth\n83\t+1.2 points on 84 seasons. Everything of value is at drain >= 5.\n84\t\n85\tNote also that drain 4-5 towns are where the unedited agent is *weakest* (control 42.9% and 42.0%,\n86\tagainst 73.0% at drain 0). The rule rescues our worst towns rather than padding our best.\n87\t\n88\tTown drain distribution over the 48 sampled towns:\n89\t\n90\t    drain    0   1   2   3   4   5   6   7\n91\t    towns    6   4   8   5   4  11   7   3\n92\t\n93\tSo **44% of towns are drain >= 5**, where the rule pays.\n94\t\n95\t`drain_min=6` was never tested and the trend is still rising at 7. 5 is the best TESTED value, not\n96\testablished as the best value.\n97\t\n98\t## How much carrot the opponents actually plant\n99\t\n100\tMeasured 2026-08-22 from `data/seed_scripts/top_guys/`: 85 replays read, 170 player-seats, counting\n101\tstanding CARROT tiles at step 600 (day 25, the decision turn) and the season peak.\n102\t\n103\t    CARROT AT DAY 25              PEAK CARROT OVER THE SEASON\n104\t        0 tiles  160 (94%)            0 tiles  145 (85%)\n105\t      >=4 tiles    7 (4%)           >=4 tiles   13 (8%)\n106\t      >=8 tiles    4 (2.4%)         >=8 tiles   11 (6%)\n107\t     >=13 tiles    3 (2%)          >=13 tiles    5 (3%)\n108\t\n109\t94% of seats hold no carrot at the decision point and 85% never plant one all season. That is the\n110\tmechanism behind the byte-identical `o3`/`o7`/`o99`: the guard had nothing to guard against.\n111\t\n112\tKeep `opp_tiles_max=7` regardless. It costs nothing, and `carrot_when_it_pays`'s own prior measured\n113\tthe `opp CA 15+` bucket at **-$510, 28% improved** -- a real tail that ~1-2% of seats reach.\n114\t\n115\tTwo limits on this sample, both real:\n116\t\n117\t- These replays are **downloaded losses** (Alex's standing rule). The\n120\t  2026-08-15 balance change put CARROT on the `hinge` price curve, so if these predate it the\n121\t  field's carrot appetite may have shifted since. Not checked.\n122\t\n123\t## Recommendation\n124\t\n125\tShip it at **`drain_min=5, opp_tiles_max=7`**, not the docstring's 4.\n126\t\n127\t`carrot_when_it_pays` lives in `kagg/search/tape_edit.py`, which is NOT in\n128\t`scripts/build_planner_submission.py::MODULES`. The shipped agent is\n129\t`kagg/search/schedule_agent.py`. Inlining the rule there is preferable to adding a module to the\n130\tbundle.\n131\t\n132\tOpen first: whether `drain_min=6` beats 5. The field is 7 leaderboard agents, all melon openers\n138\t(`melon@d3` 11-12), so `theirs` -- their standing carrot at d25 -- is always <= 3 and all three\n139\tthresholds pass.\n140\t\n141\tSix of the nine conditional arms were therefore a wash **by construction**, and it was predictable\n142\tbefore launch: `carrot_when_it_pays`'s own docstring says the opponent test \"only removes the 15+\n143\tbucket\", and the log header at 13:42 lists an opponent field with nobody in it.\n144\t\n145\tSo this run cost 12,096 seasons to answer a question 4,032 would have: control plus three drain\n146\tthresholds. There was no waiter on\nit and it was not checked once in five hours, though it printed a progress line every 200 seasons.\n\nThe arm was analysed only after the numbers were in hand, and a McNemar z was quoted before anyone\nhad read what the arm does. {"parentUuid":"8be636c6-124b-4ca4-814f-505ea483c8ca","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"74a006da-e2d3-4f09-8619-d25d7592b2b7","agentId":"a76e037eedc47105d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KKe9X89z8z7kzing4EFrDT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The measured economics\n2\t\n3\tFilename kept because `CLAUDE.md` and `kagg/agents/heuristic.py` point at it.\n4\tIt started as the answer to verification question 5 and is now the whole measured economics of the\n5\tgame: yields, drain, allocation, and selling into a shared book.\n6\t\n7\tEvery number here came from a measurement, and each one names what produced it.\n8\t`kagg/economics/` is validated against live episodes step for step and product for product in\n9\t`tests/test_market_model.py`; `tests/test_production.py` re-measures the yield table on every run.\n10\t\n11\t> **Version caveat, added 2026-08-09.** §1 (yields) and §3-5 (supply, allocation, storage) are\n12\t> version-independent — they come from per-tile production rules and `MARKET_PARAMS`, neither of\n13\t> which changed in 1.32.6. **§2 (town drain) and the market-ceiling table in §4 were measured before\n14\t> the 1.32.6 town rebalance and are now wrong by up to 4.67x (melon)** — `townCenterSellInterval`\n15\t> doubled from 12 to 24 and the late-season demand ramp was deleted. Use\n16\t> `docs/the_market_2026-08-09.md` §2 for the corrected 1.32.6 drain table, and\n17\t> `docs/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md` for the corrected per-head animal\n18\t> saturation and the melon-planning bug the old §2/§4 numbers caused.\n19\t>\n20\t> **2026-08-11 addendum.** `docs/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md` supersedes §2/§4 again, this\n21\t> time with a direct measurement off 87 tapes' closing inventory rather than a computed table.\n22\t\n23\t---\n24\t\n25\t## 1. Yields — the table in the competition docs is wrong\n26\t\n27\t`_daily_refresh_plants` stops producing once `production_count > max_yield`, so an **ongoing** crop\n28\ttile yields `max_yield` units for its *entire life* and then dies. The Object Types\n29\t\"Max yield / tile / DAY\" column is a lifetime total, not a rate.\n30\t\n31\tMeasured season output from one tile, optimal single-farmer play, no fertilizer:\n32\t\n33\t| Crop | doc claimed /tile/day | measured /tile/season | measured /tile/day | doc overstated by |\n34\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n35\t| Wheat | 1.5 | 28 | 0.93 | 1.6x |\n36\t| Carrot | 1.333 | 29 | 0.97 | 1.4x |\n37\t| Tomato | 4.0 | 8 | 0.27 | **15x** |\n38\t| Strawberry | 2.0 | 6 | 0.20 | **10x** |\n39\t| Melon | 0.5 | 12 | 0.40 | 1.25x |\n40\t| Egg | 2.0 | 26 | 0.87 | 2.3x |\n41\t| Milk | 1.0 | 11 | 0.37 | 2.7x |\n42\t| Wool | 0.67 | 8 | 0.27 | 2.5x |\n43\t\n44\tThe error ran in exactly the direction that made premium crops look like the prize, and it cost a\n45\tday-1 wrong turn. Animals are the exception in the other direction: no `production_count` cap, so a\n46\tgoose yields 26 eggs a season against a `max_held` of 4.\n47\t\n48\tFertilizer doubles ongoing-crop output (tomato 8→16, strawberry 6→12) and lifts wheat 28→42.\n49\tBuying it at $100 does not pay; **collecting it free from animals does** — `COLLECT_FERTILIZER`\n50\tcosts one action, once per animal per day, and is worth ~$85.\n51\t\n52\t---\n53\t\n54\t## 2. Town drain — fixed, large, and randomly scheduled\n55\t\n56\tNothing either player does changes the drain. Measured over a full season (seed 11; totals move\n57\tonly a few percent across seeds):\n58\t\n59\t| Product | Shops | Season drain | Price at zero player sales | x base |\n60\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n61\t| Wheat | 5 | 662 | $51 | 2.04x |\n62\t| Carrot | 2 | 410 | $42 | 1.20x |\n63\t| Tomato | 2 | 410 | $109 | 1.82x |\n64\t| Strawberry | 4 | 536 | $314 | 2.62x |\n65\t| Melon | **0** | 140 | $293 | 1.17x |\n66\t| Egg | 2 | 320 | $69 | 1.38x |\n67\t| Milk | 3 | 446 | $344 | 2.15x |\n68\t| Wool | 1 | 320 | $250 | 1.25x |\n69\t\n70\t**Melon appears in no shop.** Its only consumer is the town centre, so its drain is the smallest,\n71\tis exactly 140 on every seed, and never grows.\n72\t\n73\t### The drain does not exist from day 0, and this is the largest correction to the model\n74\t\n75\tEight shops open on days 3, 6, 9 … 24, and **which shop lands on which day is drawn per game**\n76\t(`jax_sim.random_unlock_days`):\n77\t\n78\t```\n79\tseed 0: [18, 21,  3,  9,  6, 15, 12, 24]\n80\tseed 1: [ 6,  9, 15, 21, 12,  3, 18, 24]\n81\tseed 2: [21, 24, 12, 15, 18,  6,  3,  9]\n82\t```\n83\t\n84\tA product has no shop demand at all until one of its shops opens, so shop count is a **risk**\n85\tmeasure and the objective is market size **times robustness**.\n86\t`scheduler.expected_first_demand_day` computes the wait exactly, as the minimum of `k` draws\n87\twithout replacement: wheat 4.5 days, strawberry 5.4, milk 6.8, **wool 13.5**.\n88\tWool is a $56,800 market served by one shop that can open on day 24; a sheep bought early is money\n89\tasleep, which is why the leader under-commits to it.\n90\t\n91\t**The opponent sells into the same inventory**, so every drain above is roughly halved in a\n92\tcontested game.\n93\t\n94\t---\n95\t\n96\t## 3. Supply — one quadrant against that drain\n97\t\n98\t25 tiles is the free NW quadrant, i.e. the commitment available without buying land.\n99\t\n100\t| Crop | units/tile | 25 tiles | drain | supply/drain | tiles to saturate |\n101\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n102\t| Wheat | 28 | 700 | 662 | 1.06x | 23.6 |\n103\t| Carrot | 29 | 725 | 410 | 1.77x | 14.1 |\n104\t| Tomato | 8 | 200 | 410 | **0.49x** | 51.2 |\n105\t| Strawberry | 6 | 150 | 536 | **0.28x** | 89.3 |\n106\t| Melon | 12 | 300 | 140 | 2.14x | 11.7 |\n107\t| Egg | 26 | 650 | 320 | 2.03x | 12.3 |\n108\t| Milk | 11 | 275 | 446 | **0.62x** | 40.5 |\n109\t| Wool | 8 | 200 | 320 | **0.62x** | 40.0 |\n110\t\n111\tStrawberry needs 89 tiles to saturate town demand — three and a half quadrants and $8,933 of seed\n112\tagainst a $3,000 start. Tomato needs 51 tiles, milk 40 cows, wool 40 sheep.\n113\t**None of these is reachable, which is precisely why their prices hold.**\n114\tWheat, carrot, melon and egg *can* be oversupplied by a single quadrant; those are the goods where\n115\tdumping discipline matters.\n116\t\n117\t---\n118\t\n119\t## 4. The allocation is arithmetic, not search\n120\t\n121\t`scripts/allocation_calculator.py` computes the target allocation from `chains.DRAIN_UNITS` and\n122\t`economics/production.py` with no fitted constants. Fill each market to its drain, ranked by\n123\trevenue per unit-day:\n124\t\n125\t```\n126\t     product   base   drain      market $\n127\t        MILK    160     500        78,432   <- the biggest market in the game\n128\t  STRAWBERRY    120     572        66,586\n129\t        WOOL    200     284        56,736\n130\t       MELON    250     140        35,000\n131\t      TOMATO     60     374        21,574\n132\t         EGG     50     338        16,582\n133\t       WHEAT     25     662        15,986   <- seventh of eight\n134\t      CARROT     35     338        11,484\n135\t       TOTAL                      302,380\n136\t```\n137\t\n138\tDivide each drain by per-season yield and you recover the leader's farm without searching anything:\n139\t\n140\t| | computed | leader runs |\n141\t|---|---|---|\n142\t| cows | 500 / 66 = **7.6** | **8** |\n143\t| sheep | 284 / 48 = **5.9** | **6** |\n144\t| geese | worst pen, smallest market | **0** |\n145\t| strawberry | 572 / 12 = **47.7 tiles** | **41** |\n146\t| melon | 140 / 12 = **11.7 tiles** | **20** |\n147\t\n148\t**Why this was invisible until 2026-08-05:** `DRAIN_UNITS` held the five crops only, so **$151,750\n149\tof demand — including milk, the largest single market — was missing from every allocation argument\n150\tthis project made.** The numbers were sitting in `data/logs/market_ceiling.log` the whole time.\n151\t\n152\tThe one thing the calculator gets badly wrong is **wheat**: it computes 0 tiles against the\n153\tleader's 40, because wheat is an **input**. Fourteen pens eat ~420 units a season ($10,500 of feed)\n154\tand wheat is the only crop returning cash by day 3. A revenue ranking scores it last.\n155\t\n156\t---\n157\t\n158\t## 5. Storage caps the timing prize\n159\t\n160\tThere is **no long-term storage**. 100 items overnight, overflow destroyed; carried inventory is\n161\tuncapped during a day but emptied every night; an ongoing tile holds at most `max_yield` for about a\n162\tday before decay. All pinned in `tests/test_storage.py`.\n163\t\n164\tComparing an even daily drip against the latest schedule storage actually permits:\n165\t\n166\t| Crop | qty | drip $/unit | deferred $/unit | gain | forced out early | held to end |\n167\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n168\t| Wheat | 700 | 21.7 | 27.4 | 1.26x | 600 | 100 |\n169\t| Carrot | 725 | 19.6 | 22.4 | 1.14x | 575 | 150 |\n170\t| Tomato | 200 | 77.1 | 90.1 | 1.17x | 100 | 100 |\n171\t| Strawberry | 150 | 257.2 | 300.3 | 1.17x | **0** | 150 |\n172\t| Melon | 300 | 175.5 | 203.2 | 1.16x | 200 | 100 |\n173\t\n174\tThe mechanism is real — town drain runs whether or not anyone sells, so an untouched market drifts\n175\tinto scarcity — but the gain is a **uniform 1.14x-1.26x**, not the 1.5-1.75x an unlimited-storage\n176\tmodel suggests.\n177\t\n178\t> **Correction on the record.** An earlier version claimed 1.75x for wheat and called wheat the\n179\t> biggest timing win. That was computed with a schedule holding 700 units, which no shed can do.\n180\t> Wheat's real gain is 1.26x and it is the crop *most* forced into drip selling.\n181\t\n182\t**The lever is production timing, not sale timing.** A crop that matures in the last week needs no\n183\tstorage at all — strawberry shows it cleanly, with its 150 units arriving on days 16 and 29 only.\n184\t\n185\t---\n186\t\n187\t## 6. Selling into a shared book\n188\t\n189\t`market_price(item, inventory)` is a pure function of the shared book\n190\t(`reference/kaggriculture.py:177`). `MARKET_PARAMS` is a module constant and `_new_market` writes a\n191\t`params` key **only when the params are non-default**; a live game's observation carries `market`\n192\twith keys `['inventory', 'prices']` and no `params`, so the competition runs the defaults and\n193\t**we know the curve exactly**. Given `obs[\"market\"][\"inventory\"]` we can compute what every\n194\tsuccessive unit of a SELL will fetch before ordering it.\n195\t\n196\tAbove `I0` (10,000) the curve is brutally shallow for exactly the products we live on:\n197\t\n198\t| units above I0 | 0 | +20 | +50 | +100 |\n199\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n200\t| MILK | 160 | 118 | 55 | 1 |\n201\t| WOOL | 200 | 177 | 55 | 1 |\n202\t| STRAWBERRY | 120 | 82 | 24 | 1 |\n203\t| MELON | 250 | 246 | 225 | 150 |\n204\t\n205\tBoth seats add to the same inventory, so an opponent selling what we sell taxes us without ever\n206\ttouching our farm. Measured on two of our own leaderboard games:\n207\t\n208\t| game | opponent | our units sold | milk price d15-29 | final |\n209\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n210\t| 90534961 | Sophie | 1058 | 101.1 | $103,367 **lost** |\n211\t| 90536513 | Mohit Babel | 1017 | 225.1 | $128,735 **won** |\n212\t\n213\tWe sold **more** units in the game we lost. Milk alone — ~135 units at ~$124/unit worse — is ~$16.7k\n214\tof the $25.4k gap; strawberry adds ~$8.8k.\n215\t\n216\t### Two rejected arms, and why both had to fail\n217\t\n218\tBoth were **static rules against a level**, in a game whose realised price is bimodal:\n219\t\n220\t| tried | measured | why it could not work |\n221\t|---|---|---|\n222\t| refuse to sell below 0.5x base | **-$16,025** | 0.5x base for milk is $80; the price is either ~$160 (book at I0) or ~$55 (book +50). The floor blocks everything or nothing. |\n223\t| cap the quota to a day total rather than a turn | **-$2,653** | Spaces by CLOCK, not by price. A standing offer collects each shop drain; a one-shot order does not. |\n224\t\n225\tThe rule that fits the mechanism is **marginal, not floored**: walk down the computed curve and\n226\tstop at the first unit whose price falls below a reservation value. Holding has a knowable value\n227\tbecause the drain is deterministic (`kaggriculture.py:706-716`).\n228\t\n229\t**Blind spot to close first:** `scripts/agent_ladder.py` drives seat 1 from a fixed replay, so the\n230\tonly contention it can ever measure is whatever that one tape did. Both negatives above were\n231\tmeasured against a non-contesting opponent. This is the \"never evaluate against `starter` alone\"\n232\tlesson in a new costume, and it has now cost a measured $25k in a live game.\n233\t\n234\t### Market manipulation and timing are not where the money is\n235\t\n236\t`scripts/measure_true_drain.py` on a real leader replay: the town inventory never saturates, so the\n237\t\"everyone sells wheat on day 28 and the price crashes\" tragedy-of-the-commons does not occur.\n238\tShifting only the sell days moves money at most **+3.6% and the win rate not at all**.\n239\tMarket timing, front-running and withholding together are ~3% of money and 0% of wins.\n240\tThe leader's own gaps between sell days are 1.0 (wheat, fertilizer) to 1.4 (wool) days — he sells\n241\tnearly daily, because **a fixed tape cannot wait for a demand event it cannot observe**.\n242\tDaily is the robust choice, not the lazy one.\n243\t\n244\t---\n245\t\n246\t## 7. What this changes\n247\t\n248\t- The strategy is neither \"wheat volume\" nor \"premium timing\". It is *pick crops by revenue per\n249\t  tile-day against measured yields, size each to its drain, and weight by how early its shop is\n250\t  likely to open.*\n251\t- **Animals are the only uncapped producer**, and they convert wheat into a product worth 4-8x the\n252\t  wheat. Milk at $344 with zero player sales is the strongest premium position on the board.\n253\t  See `experiment_log.md` for the four separate times \"animals do not pay\" turned out to be a\n254\t  limitation of our own code.\n255\t- **Melon is the least scalable product in the game** — no shop, fixed 140 units — and our own\n256\t  searches built a monoculture on it three times.\n257\t- There is no large untapped market left to discover. The whole market is $302,380, \"1.0x if split\n258\t  evenly between two players\", and the leader banks $139,121 — he captures nearly his entire share.\n259\t  The remaining edge is efficiency and robustness.\n260\t\n261\t## Still not verified\n262\t\n263\t- **Intra-day throughput.** The deferred schedules in §5 sell up to 150 units in one day, which is\n264\t  legal only because carried inventory is uncapped and selling from the shed frees room to drop\n265\t  more. Whether those instalments are routable inside the action budget is untested, so every\n266\t  deferred figure is an upper bound.\n267\t- **The CARE bonus** is excluded from every yield above, so they are floors rather than ceilings.\n268\t"}]},"uuid":"429db53f-e500-4c4c-935d-d47b72808ace","timestamp":"2026-08-24T11:38:32.037Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/docs/economics_q5_findings.md","content":"# The measured economics\n\nFilename kept because `CLAUDE.md` and `kagg/agents/heuristic.py` point at it.\nIt started as the answer to verification question 5 and is now the whole measured economics of the\ngame: yields, drain, allocation, and selling into a shared book.\n\nEvery number here came from a measurement, and each one names what produced it.\n`kagg/economics/` is validated against live episodes step for step and product for product in\n`tests/test_market_model.py`; `tests/test_production.py` re-measures the yield table on every run.\n\n> **Version caveat, added 2026-08-09.** §1 (yields) and §3-5 (supply, allocation, storage) are\n> version-independent — they come from per-tile production rules and `MARKET_PARAMS`, neither of\n> which changed in 1.32.6. **§2 (town drain) and the market-ceiling table in §4 were measured before\n> the 1.32.6 town rebalance and are now wrong by up to 4.67x (melon)** — `townCenterSellInterval`\n> doubled from 12 to 24 and the late-season demand ramp was deleted. Use\n> `docs/the_market_2026-08-09.md` §2 for the corrected 1.32.6 drain table, and\n> `docs/what_saturates_and_what_does_not_2026-08-09.md` for the corrected per-head animal\n> saturation and the melon-planning bug the old §2/§4 numbers caused.\n>\n> **2026-08-11 addendum.** `docs/what_the_town_will_actually_eat.md` supersedes §2/§4 again, this\n> time with a direct measurement off 87 tapes' closing inventory rather than a computed table.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Yields — the table in the competition docs is wrong\n\n`_daily_refresh_plants` stops producing once `production_count > max_yield`, so an **ongoing** crop\ntile yields `max_yield` units for its *entire life* and then dies. The Object Types\n\"Max yield / tile / DAY\" column is a lifetime total, not a rate.\n\nMeasured season output from one tile, optimal single-farmer play, no fertilizer:\n\n| Crop | doc claimed /tile/day | measured /tile/season | measured /tile/day | doc overstated by |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Wheat | 1.5 | 28 | 0.93 | 1.6x |\n| Carrot | 1.333 | 29 | 0.97 | 1.4x |\n| Tomato | 4.0 | 8 | 0.27 | **15x** |\n| Strawberry | 2.0 | 6 | 0.20 | **10x** |\n| Melon | 0.5 | 12 | 0.40 | 1.25x |\n| Egg | 2.0 | 26 | 0.87 | 2.3x |\n| Milk | 1.0 | 11 | 0.37 | 2.7x |\n| Wool | 0.67 | 8 | 0.27 | 2.5x |\n\nThe error ran in exactly the direction that made premium crops look like the prize, and it cost a\nday-1 wrong turn. Animals are the exception in the other direction: no `production_count` cap, so a\ngoose yields 26 eggs a season against a `max_held` of 4.\n\nFertilizer doubles ongoing-crop output (tomato 8→16, strawberry 6→12) and lifts wheat 28→42.\nBuying it at $100 does not pay; **collecting it free from animals does** — `COLLECT_FERTILIZER`\ncosts one action, once per animal per day, and is worth ~$85.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Town drain — fixed, large, and randomly scheduled\n\nNothing either player does changes the drain. Measured over a full season (seed 11; totals move\nonly a few percent across seeds):\n\n| Product | Shops | Season drain | Price at zero player sales | x base |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Wheat | 5 | 662 | $51 | 2.04x |\n| Carrot | 2 | 410 | $42 | 1.20x |\n| Tomato | 2 | 410 | $109 | 1.82x |\n| Strawberry | 4 | 536 | $314 | 2.62x |\n| Melon | **0** | 140 | $293 | 1.17x |\n| Egg | 2 | 320 | $69 | 1.38x |\n| Milk | 3 | 446 | $344 | 2.15x |\n| Wool | 1 | 320 | $250 | 1.25x |\n\n**Melon appears in no shop.** Its only consumer is the town centre, so its drain is the smallest,\nis exactly 140 on every seed, and never grows.\n\n### The drain does not exist from day 0, and this is the largest correction to the model\n\nEight shops open on days 3, 6, 9 … 24, and **which shop lands on which day is drawn per game**\n(`jax_sim.random_unlock_days`):\n\n```\nseed 0: [18, 21,  3,  9,  6, 15, 12, 24]\nseed 1: [ 6,  9, 15, 21, 12,  3, 18, 24]\nseed 2: [21, 24, 12, 15, 18,  6,  3,  9]\n```\n\nA product has no shop demand at all until one of its shops opens, so shop count is a **risk**\nmeasure and the objective is market size **times robustness**.\n`scheduler.expected_first_demand_day` computes the wait exactly, as the minimum of `k` draws\nwithout replacement: wheat 4.5 days, strawberry 5.4, milk 6.8, **wool 13.5**.\nWool is a $56,800 market served by one shop that can open on day 24; a sheep bought early is money\nasleep, which is why the leader under-commits to it.\n\n**The opponent sells into the same inventory**, so every drain above is roughly halved in a\ncontested game.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Supply — one quadrant against that drain\n\n25 tiles is the free NW quadrant, i.e. the commitment available without buying land.\n\n| Crop | units/tile | 25 tiles | drain | supply/drain | tiles to saturate |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Wheat | 28 | 700 | 662 | 1.06x | 23.6 |\n| Carrot | 29 | 725 | 410 | 1.77x | 14.1 |\n| Tomato | 8 | 200 | 410 | **0.49x** | 51.2 |\n| Strawberry | 6 | 150 | 536 | **0.28x** | 89.3 |\n| Melon | 12 | 300 | 140 | 2.14x | 11.7 |\n| Egg | 26 | 650 | 320 | 2.03x | 12.3 |\n| Milk | 11 | 275 | 446 | **0.62x** | 40.5 |\n| Wool | 8 | 200 | 320 | **0.62x** | 40.0 |\n\nStrawberry needs 89 tiles to saturate town demand — three and a half quadrants and $8,933 of seed\nagainst a $3,000 start. Tomato needs 51 tiles, milk 40 cows, wool 40 sheep.\n**None of these is reachable, which is precisely why their prices hold.**\nWheat, carrot, melon and egg *can* be oversupplied by a single quadrant; those are the goods where\ndumping discipline matters.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The allocation is arithmetic, not search\n\n`scripts/allocation_calculator.py` computes the target allocation from `chains.DRAIN_UNITS` and\n`economics/production.py` with no fitted constants. Fill each market to its drain, ranked by\nrevenue per unit-day:\n\n```\n     product   base   drain      market $\n        MILK    160     500        78,432   <- the biggest market in the game\n  STRAWBERRY    120     572        66,586\n        WOOL    200     284        56,736\n       MELON    250     140        35,000\n      TOMATO     60     374        21,574\n         EGG     50     338        16,582\n       WHEAT     25     662        15,986   <- seventh of eight\n      CARROT     35     338        11,484\n       TOTAL                      302,380\n```\n\nDivide each drain by per-season yield and you recover the leader's farm without searching anything:\n\n| | computed | leader runs |\n|---|---|---|\n| cows | 500 / 66 = **7.6** | **8** |\n| sheep | 284 / 48 = **5.9** | **6** |\n| geese | worst pen, smallest market | **0** |\n| strawberry | 572 / 12 = **47.7 tiles** | **41** |\n| melon | 140 / 12 = **11.7 tiles** | **20** |\n\n**Why this was invisible until 2026-08-05:** `DRAIN_UNITS` held the five crops only, so **$151,750\nof demand — including milk, the largest single market — was missing from every allocation argument\nthis project made.** The numbers were sitting in `data/logs/market_ceiling.log` the whole time.\n\nThe one thing the calculator gets badly wrong is **wheat**: it computes 0 tiles against the\nleader's 40, because wheat is an **input**. Fourteen pens eat ~420 units a season ($10,500 of feed)\nand wheat is the only crop returning cash by day 3. A revenue ranking scores it last.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Storage caps the timing prize\n\nThere is **no long-term storage**. 100 items overnight, overflow destroyed; carried inventory is\nuncapped during a day but emptied every night; an ongoing tile holds at most `max_yield` for about a\nday before decay. All pinned in `tests/test_storage.py`.\n\nComparing an even daily drip against the latest schedule storage actually permits:\n\n| Crop | qty | drip $/unit | deferred $/unit | gain | forced out early | held to end |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Wheat | 700 | 21.7 | 27.4 | 1.26x | 600 | 100 |\n| Carrot | 725 | 19.6 | 22.4 | 1.14x | 575 | 150 |\n| Tomato | 200 | 77.1 | 90.1 | 1.17x | 100 | 100 |\n| Strawberry | 150 | 257.2 | 300.3 | 1.17x | **0** | 150 |\n| Melon | 300 | 175.5 | 203.2 | 1.16x | 200 | 100 |\n\nThe mechanism is real — town drain runs whether or not anyone sells, so an untouched market drifts\ninto scarcity — but the gain is a **uniform 1.14x-1.26x**, not the 1.5-1.75x an unlimited-storage\nmodel suggests.\n\n> **Correction on the record.** An earlier version claimed 1.75x for wheat and called wheat the\n> biggest timing win. That was computed with a schedule holding 700 units, which no shed can do.\n> Wheat's real gain is 1.26x and it is the crop *most* forced into drip selling.\n\n**The lever is production timing, not sale timing.** A crop that matures in the last week needs no\nstorage at all — strawberry shows it cleanly, with its 150 units arriving on days 16 and 29 only.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Selling into a shared book\n\n`market_price(item, inventory)` is a pure function of the shared book\n(`reference/kaggriculture.py:177`). `MARKET_PARAMS` is a module constant and `_new_market` writes a\n`params` key **only when the params are non-default**; a live game's observation carries `market`\nwith keys `['inventory', 'prices']` and no `params`, so the competition runs the defaults and\n**we know the curve exactly**. Given `obs[\"market\"][\"inventory\"]` we can compute what every\nsuccessive unit of a SELL will fetch before ordering it.\n\nAbove `I0` (10,000) the curve is brutally shallow for exactly the products we live on:\n\n| units above I0 | 0 | +20 | +50 | +100 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| MILK | 160 | 118 | 55 | 1 |\n| WOOL | 200 | 177 | 55 | 1 |\n| STRAWBERRY | 120 | 82 | 24 | 1 |\n| MELON | 250 | 246 | 225 | 150 |\n\nBoth seats add to the same inventory, so an opponent selling what we sell taxes us without ever\ntouching our farm. Measured on two of our own leaderboard games:\n\n| game | opponent | our units sold | milk price d15-29 | final |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 90534961 | Sophie | 1058 | 101.1 | $103,367 **lost** |\n| 90536513 | Mohit Babel | 1017 | 225.1 | $128,735 **won** |\n\nWe sold **more** units in the game we lost. Milk alone — ~135 units at ~$124/unit worse — is ~$16.7k\nof the $25.4k gap; strawberry adds ~$8.8k.\n\n### Two rejected arms, and why both had to fail\n\nBoth were **static rules against a level**, in a game whose realised price is bimodal:\n\n| tried | measured | why it could not work |\n|---|---|---|\n| refuse to sell below 0.5x base | **-$16,025** | 0.5x base for milk is $80; the price is either ~$160 (book at I0) or ~$55 (book +50). The floor blocks everything or nothing. |\n| cap the quota to a day total rather than a turn | **-$2,653** | Spaces by CLOCK, not by price. A standing offer collects each shop drain; a one-shot order does not. |\n\nThe rule that fits the mechanism is **marginal, not floored**: walk down the computed curve and\nstop at the first unit whose price falls below a reservation value. Holding has a knowable value\nbecause the drain is deterministic (`kaggriculture.py:706-716`).\n\n**Blind spot to close first:** `scripts/agent_ladder.py` drives seat 1 from a fixed replay, so the\nonly contention it can ever measure is whatever that one tape did. Both negatives above were\nmeasured against a non-contesting opponent. This is the \"never evaluate against `starter` alone\"\nlesson in a new costume, and it has now cost a measured $25k in a live game.\n\n### Market manipulation and timing are not where the money is\n\n`scripts/measure_true_drain.py` on a real leader replay: the town inventory never saturates, so the\n\"everyone sells wheat on day 28 and the price crashes\" tragedy-of-the-commons does not occur.\nShifting only the sell days moves money at most **+3.6% and the win rate not at all**.\nMarket timing, front-running and withholding together are ~3% of money and 0% of wins.\nThe leader's own gaps between sell days are 1.0 (wheat, fertilizer) to 1.4 (wool) days — he sells\nnearly daily, because **a fixed tape cannot wait for a demand event it cannot observe**.\nDaily is the robust choice, not the lazy one.\n\n---\n\n## 7. What this changes\n\n- The strategy is neither \"wheat volume\" nor \"premium timing\". It is *pick crops by revenue per\n  tile-day against measured yields, size each to its drain, and weight by how early its shop is\n  likely to open.*\n- **Animals are the only uncapped producer**, and they convert wheat into a product worth 4-8x the\n  wheat. Milk at $344 with zero player sales is the strongest premium position on the board.\n  See `experiment_log.md` for the four separate times \"animals do not pay\" turned out to be a\n  limitation of our own code.\n- **Melon is the least scalable product in the game** — no shop, fixed 140 units — and our own\n  searches built a monoculture on it three times.\n- There is no large untapped market left to discover. The whole market is $302,380, \"1.0x if split\n  evenly between two players\", and the leader banks $139,121 — he captures nearly his entire share.\n  The remaining edge is efficiency and robustness.\n\n## Still not verified\n\n- **Intra-day throughput.** The deferred schedules in §5 sell up to 150 units in one day, which is\n  legal only because carried inventory is uncapped and selling from the shed frees room to drop\n  more. Whether those instalments are routable inside the action budget is untested, so every\n  deferred figure is an upper bound.\n- **The CARE bonus** is excluded from every yield above, so they are floors rather than ceilings.\n","numLines":268,"startLine":1,"totalLines":268}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8be636c6-124b-4ca4-814f-505ea483c8ca","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}
