{"parentUuid":"d9381b91-4553-49d9-8d4d-5d830e4f2676","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"74a006da-e2d3-4f09-8619-d25d7592b2b7","agentId":"a76e037eedc47105d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01K9e9ByWuKwf1S6Yc5qhTQR","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The second cow is unbuyable, and that one missing animal is what desynchronises the crew\n2\t\n3\t2026-08-22. Measured on this Mac, `.venv`, checkpoint\n4\t`data/dagger_tape/d01gate-20260822-185347/latest.pkl` round 14, vocabulary\n5\t`data/il/bundle_vocab_teacher.json` (148 entries), tape `(False, False)` seat 0, 48 turns, the\n6\tSTUDENT driving every turn. Log: `data/logs/probe_greedy_playout.log`.\n7\t\n8\tSame class of defect as `docs/the_vocabulary_labels_the_opening_as_nothing_2026-08-22.md`: an action\n9\tthe teacher takes that the representation cannot express.\n10\t\n11\t## It does hire, and the crew matches exactly\n12\t\n13\t```\n14\tturn  crew(o/t)   bank(o/t)         <- greedy decode, student driving\n15\t   0    1/1      3000/3000\n16\t   1    6/6       884/13\n17\t  23    6/6       916/16\n18\t  24    1/1       916/16\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\tFive hires at turn 0, six units all through day 0, and one unit on day 1 -- the tape does the same,\n22\tbecause the hands do not survive the day boundary and it cannot afford to re-hire until day 2.\n23\tSo \"does it hire and route the crew\" is answered: yes, and the crew SIZE is exact.\n24\t\n25\t## What it gets wrong: one cow and one sheep, not two\n26\t\n27\t```\n28\tours [BUY_SEED MELON 12, BUY_SEED WHEAT 7, BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT 6,\n29\t      BUY_ANIMAL COW 1, BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP 1, HIRE x5]\n30\ttape [HIRE x5, BUY_ANIMAL COW 2, BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP 2,\n31\t      BUY_SEED WHEAT 7, BUY_SEED MELON 12, BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT 6]\n32\t```\n33\t\n34\tIdentical but for the animal counts. COW is $400 and SHEEP is $500 (`reference/kaggriculture.py:21`),\n35\tso the missing pair is **$900** against an observed gap of $871 at turn 1.\n36\t\n37\t## Where the quantity is lost, which is NOT where the comment says\n38\t\n39\t`replays.py` blames its own `buy_animal` label:\n40\t\n41\t> \"**Binary, not a quantity.** The `buy_animal` head is `N_ANIMALS x 2` ... the only values it can\n42\t> express are 0 and 1.\"\n43\t\n44\tThat label is vestigial -- `buy_animal` is **not in `jax_act.ALL_HEADS`** and is never scored. The\n45\treal loss is upstream, in the bundle:\n46\t\n47\t- `market_bundles.bundle_of` (`:55`) emits one `(op, item)` pair **per ORDER**. `HIRE` x5 is five\n48\t  separate orders, so its multiplicity survives. `BUY_ANIMAL COW 2` is ONE order carrying a\n49\t  quantity, so it becomes one pair and the 2 is dropped.\n50\t- `to_sim_fields` (`:165`) would recover it -- it does `buy_animal[...] += qty` from a `quantities`\n51\t  vector aligned to the bundle. But the head set is `qty_sell`, `qty_buy_product`, `qty_buy_seed`.\n52\t  **There is no `qty_buy_animal`**, so nothing ever supplies that quantity and it defaults to 1.\n53\t\n54\tSo the policy cannot say \"buy two cows\" at any width or any amount of training.\n55\t\n56\t## The consequence: 0.28 unit-op agreement is a symptom, not the disease\n57\t\n58\t```\n59\tunit-op agreement, student driving, greedy   46/163 = 0.2822\n60\t                                  sampled    32/163 = 0.1963\n61\t```\n62\t\n63\tThe mismatches concentrate in slot 3 -- the hand the tape sends for the second cow:\n64\t\n65\t```\n66\tturn 1  ours slot3 PASS   tape WEST\n67\tturn 2  ours slot3 PASS   tape NORTH\n68\tturn 3  ours slot3 PASS   tape PICKUP COW 1\n69\t```\n70\t\n71\tThere is no second cow, so that unit has nothing to fetch and passes; its tour desynchronises and\n72\tnever recovers. The top confusions are exactly that shape: `PASS->EAST 7`, `PASS->WEST 7`,\n73\t`NORTH->PASS 5`. Read as a movement failure this would send the work in entirely the wrong\n74\tdirection.\n75\t\n76\t## Greedy beats sampled, decisively\n77\t\n78\t```\n79\t                       unit-op agreement   final money   turn-0 market\n80\tgreedy                     0.2822              916       the opening, animals short\n81\tsampled                    0.1963             2988       SELL MILK 3, SELL STRAWBERRY 6,\n82\t                                                         SELL WHEAT 3, SELL WOOL 4, HIRE x5\n83\t```\n84\t\n85\tThe sampled decode spends **$12 of $3,000** across the whole of day 0 and tries to sell four goods\n86\tit does not own. Greedy spends $2,084. On teacher-driven states the same checkpoint reads\n87\t`greedy bundle 1.0000` against `sample bundle 0.9635`, and over a sequence that must be exact,\n88\t`0.96^719` is zero while `1.00^719` is one.\n89\t\n90\t`neural.py` refuses argmax because independent per-head marginals compose into \"never sell\". That\n91\treasoning was correct for six independent market heads and does not apply to one `bundle`\n92\tcategorical, which is a joint choice already. The rule has expired; this is the measurement.\n93\t\n94\t## The audit that should have found all of this on day one\n95\t\n96\tFinding these by symptom is the wrong method: the vocabulary blanking, then the animal count, each\n97\tdiscovered only after it had corrupted a run. There is one question with one answer --\n98\t\n99\t> encode every teacher action to labels, decode it straight back, and compare.\n100\t\n101\tA perfect student emits the label, so anything that does not survive the round trip is an action\n102\t**no policy can play at any width after any amount of training**. No network is involved.\n103\tProbe: `scratchpad/probe_roundtrip_audit.py`, logs `data/logs/probe_roundtrip_audit*.log`.\n104\t\n105\tOver all 2,876 teacher turns of the four tapes:\n106\t\n107\t```\n108\t                        before   after\n109\tMARKET does not survive    203 ->    4     (0.3% of 1451 trading turns)\n110\t   wrong ORDER SET         179 ->    0     the vocabulary\n111\t   wrong QUANTITY animal    20 ->    0     the second cow\n112\t   wrong QUANTITY SELL       4 ->    4     left, see below\n113\tUNIT ops                   587 ->   36\n114\t   PICKUP/PLACE quantity   551 ->    0     the bucket ladder\n115\t   DROP arity               36 ->   36     cosmetic\n116\t```\n117\t\n118\t`DROP` is cosmetic: `reference/kaggriculture.py:343-355` drops the unit's entire inventory and never\n119\treads `action[1]` or `action[2]`, so `['DROP','WHEAT',3]` and `['DROP']` are the same action.\n120\t\n121\t## What was changed\n122\t\n123\t- **`bundle_of`** repeats an animal pair per unit, as `HIRE` already was. `quantities_of` and\n124\t  `from_sim_fields` follow, so the documented inverse still holds.\n125\t- **`orders_from_heads` coalesces those repeats back into one order carrying the count.** This is\n126\t  the part the audit caught and the tests would not have: a turn holds TEN orders and the opening\n127\t  uses exactly ten, so emitting `COW 1` twice instead of `COW 2` pushed it to twelve and truncated\n128\t  two HIREs off the tail. The first version of this fix traded the second cow for two hands.\n129\t- **`UNIT_QTY_BUCKETS`** `[1, 2, 5, 10]` -> exact `1..16`. Its own comment called it \"unexamined,\n130\t  not agreed\"; examined, it cost 551 unit ops. 12 is the largest the teachers ask for.\n131\t- The vocabulary was rebuilt for the new animal symbols: **149 entries**, 149/149 coverage.\n132\t\n133\t**The `qty` head changed shape**, so existing checkpoints cannot be warm-started for it. At 48 turns\n134\ta cold start is cheap; a 719-turn run would want the same by-shape head reset the vocabulary change\n135\talready uses.\n136\t\n137\t**This audit covers ON-TAPE states only** -- it is collected with the teacher driving. As beta\n138\tanneals the teacher may emit bundles it never emits on-tape. That is no longer silent: `encode_action`\n139\treturns `w_bundle_miss` and `dagger_tape` aborts at round 0 on any miss.\n140\t\n141\t## The SELL clamp, left deliberately\n142\t\n143\t```\n144\tSELL WHEAT 72 -> 63     4 turns, all day 29 hour 0\n145\t```\n146\t\n147\t`MAX_QTY = 64` and the class index IS the quantity, so raising it to 128 doubles `qty_sell`,\n148\t`qty_buy_product` and `qty_buy_seed`, and all three must then be reset and retrained. That is an\n149\tarchitecture change for 4 turns of 1451, costing 9 unsold wheat on the final liquidation -- order of\n150\t$200-350 against a ~$150k season. The other two gaps cost the opening and 551 unit ops. Recorded\n151\there so the trade is visible rather than forgotten.\n152\t\n153\t## The fix, two options (as considered at the time)\n154\t\n155\t1. **Repeat the animal pair per unit in `bundle_of`, as `HIRE` already is.** `COW 2` becomes two\n156\t   `(BUY_ANIMAL, COW)` pairs, and `to_sim_fields`'s `+= qty` with the default of 1 then yields 2.\n157\t   No architecture change, no new head; `from_sim_fields` needs the matching `* n` so the two stay\n158\t   inverses, and `tests/test_a_market_bundle_survives_the_round_trip.py` covers that. `max_held` is\n159\t   6, so the vocabulary grows by a handful of entries at most.\n160\t2. **Add a `qty_buy_animal` head.** Symmetric with the other three, but it is a shape change: every\n161\t   checkpoint needs migrating and the head has to be trained from scratch.\n162\t\n163\t(1) is cheaper, matches the existing treatment of `HIRE`, and needs no checkpoint surgery.\n164\t"}]},"uuid":"9f1c3e56-8323-445c-b05f-4dd8acff4203","timestamp":"2026-08-24T11:38:32.250Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture/docs/the_second_cow_cannot_be_bought_2026-08-22.md","content":"# The second cow is unbuyable, and that one missing animal is what desynchronises the crew\n\n2026-08-22. Measured on this Mac, `.venv`, checkpoint\n`data/dagger_tape/d01gate-20260822-185347/latest.pkl` round 14, vocabulary\n`data/il/bundle_vocab_teacher.json` (148 entries), tape `(False, False)` seat 0, 48 turns, the\nSTUDENT driving every turn. Log: `data/logs/probe_greedy_playout.log`.\n\nSame class of defect as `docs/the_vocabulary_labels_the_opening_as_nothing_2026-08-22.md`: an action\nthe teacher takes that the representation cannot express.\n\n## It does hire, and the crew matches exactly\n\n```\nturn  crew(o/t)   bank(o/t)         <- greedy decode, student driving\n   0    1/1      3000/3000\n   1    6/6       884/13\n  23    6/6       916/16\n  24    1/1       916/16\n```\n\nFive hires at turn 0, six units all through day 0, and one unit on day 1 -- the tape does the same,\nbecause the hands do not survive the day boundary and it cannot afford to re-hire until day 2.\nSo \"does it hire and route the crew\" is answered: yes, and the crew SIZE is exact.\n\n## What it gets wrong: one cow and one sheep, not two\n\n```\nours [BUY_SEED MELON 12, BUY_SEED WHEAT 7, BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT 6,\n      BUY_ANIMAL COW 1, BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP 1, HIRE x5]\ntape [HIRE x5, BUY_ANIMAL COW 2, BUY_ANIMAL SHEEP 2,\n      BUY_SEED WHEAT 7, BUY_SEED MELON 12, BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT 6]\n```\n\nIdentical but for the animal counts. COW is $400 and SHEEP is $500 (`reference/kaggriculture.py:21`),\nso the missing pair is **$900** against an observed gap of $871 at turn 1.\n\n## Where the quantity is lost, which is NOT where the comment says\n\n`replays.py` blames its own `buy_animal` label:\n\n> \"**Binary, not a quantity.** The `buy_animal` head is `N_ANIMALS x 2` ... the only values it can\n> express are 0 and 1.\"\n\nThat label is vestigial -- `buy_animal` is **not in `jax_act.ALL_HEADS`** and is never scored. The\nreal loss is upstream, in the bundle:\n\n- `market_bundles.bundle_of` (`:55`) emits one `(op, item)` pair **per ORDER**. `HIRE` x5 is five\n  separate orders, so its multiplicity survives. `BUY_ANIMAL COW 2` is ONE order carrying a\n  quantity, so it becomes one pair and the 2 is dropped.\n- `to_sim_fields` (`:165`) would recover it -- it does `buy_animal[...] += qty` from a `quantities`\n  vector aligned to the bundle. But the head set is `qty_sell`, `qty_buy_product`, `qty_buy_seed`.\n  **There is no `qty_buy_animal`**, so nothing ever supplies that quantity and it defaults to 1.\n\nSo the policy cannot say \"buy two cows\" at any width or any amount of training.\n\n## The consequence: 0.28 unit-op agreement is a symptom, not the disease\n\n```\nunit-op agreement, student driving, greedy   46/163 = 0.2822\n                                  sampled    32/163 = 0.1963\n```\n\nThe mismatches concentrate in slot 3 -- the hand the tape sends for the second cow:\n\n```\nturn 1  ours slot3 PASS   tape WEST\nturn 2  ours slot3 PASS   tape NORTH\nturn 3  ours slot3 PASS   tape PICKUP COW 1\n```\n\nThere is no second cow, so that unit has nothing to fetch and passes; its tour desynchronises and\nnever recovers. The top confusions are exactly that shape: `PASS->EAST 7`, `PASS->WEST 7`,\n`NORTH->PASS 5`. Read as a movement failure this would send the work in entirely the wrong\ndirection.\n\n## Greedy beats sampled, decisively\n\n```\n                       unit-op agreement   final money   turn-0 market\ngreedy                     0.2822              916       the opening, animals short\nsampled                    0.1963             2988       SELL MILK 3, SELL STRAWBERRY 6,\n                                                         SELL WHEAT 3, SELL WOOL 4, HIRE x5\n```\n\nThe sampled decode spends **$12 of $3,000** across the whole of day 0 and tries to sell four goods\nit does not own. Greedy spends $2,084. On teacher-driven states the same checkpoint reads\n`greedy bundle 1.0000` against `sample bundle 0.9635`, and over a sequence that must be exact,\n`0.96^719` is zero while `1.00^719` is one.\n\n`neural.py` refuses argmax because independent per-head marginals compose into \"never sell\". That\nreasoning was correct for six independent market heads and does not apply to one `bundle`\ncategorical, which is a joint choice already. The rule has expired; this is the measurement.\n\n## The audit that should have found all of this on day one\n\nFinding these by symptom is the wrong method: the vocabulary blanking, then the animal count, each\ndiscovered only after it had corrupted a run. There is one question with one answer --\n\n> encode every teacher action to labels, decode it straight back, and compare.\n\nA perfect student emits the label, so anything that does not survive the round trip is an action\n**no policy can play at any width after any amount of training**. No network is involved.\nProbe: `scratchpad/probe_roundtrip_audit.py`, logs `data/logs/probe_roundtrip_audit*.log`.\n\nOver all 2,876 teacher turns of the four tapes:\n\n```\n                        before   after\nMARKET does not survive    203 ->    4     (0.3% of 1451 trading turns)\n   wrong ORDER SET         179 ->    0     the vocabulary\n   wrong QUANTITY animal    20 ->    0     the second cow\n   wrong QUANTITY SELL       4 ->    4     left, see below\nUNIT ops                   587 ->   36\n   PICKUP/PLACE quantity   551 ->    0     the bucket ladder\n   DROP arity               36 ->   36     cosmetic\n```\n\n`DROP` is cosmetic: `reference/kaggriculture.py:343-355` drops the unit's entire inventory and never\nreads `action[1]` or `action[2]`, so `['DROP','WHEAT',3]` and `['DROP']` are the same action.\n\n## What was changed\n\n- **`bundle_of`** repeats an animal pair per unit, as `HIRE` already was. `quantities_of` and\n  `from_sim_fields` follow, so the documented inverse still holds.\n- **`orders_from_heads` coalesces those repeats back into one order carrying the count.** This is\n  the part the audit caught and the tests would not have: a turn holds TEN orders and the opening\n  uses exactly ten, so emitting `COW 1` twice instead of `COW 2` pushed it to twelve and truncated\n  two HIREs off the tail. The first version of this fix traded the second cow for two hands.\n- **`UNIT_QTY_BUCKETS`** `[1, 2, 5, 10]` -> exact `1..16`. Its own comment called it \"unexamined,\n  not agreed\"; examined, it cost 551 unit ops. 12 is the largest the teachers ask for.\n- The vocabulary was rebuilt for the new animal symbols: **149 entries**, 149/149 coverage.\n\n**The `qty` head changed shape**, so existing checkpoints cannot be warm-started for it. At 48 turns\na cold start is cheap; a 719-turn run would want the same by-shape head reset the vocabulary change\nalready uses.\n\n**This audit covers ON-TAPE states only** -- it is collected with the teacher driving. As beta\nanneals the teacher may emit bundles it never emits on-tape. That is no longer silent: `encode_action`\nreturns `w_bundle_miss` and `dagger_tape` aborts at round 0 on any miss.\n\n## The SELL clamp, left deliberately\n\n```\nSELL WHEAT 72 -> 63     4 turns, all day 29 hour 0\n```\n\n`MAX_QTY = 64` and the class index IS the quantity, so raising it to 128 doubles `qty_sell`,\n`qty_buy_product` and `qty_buy_seed`, and all three must then be reset and retrained. That is an\narchitecture change for 4 turns of 1451, costing 9 unsold wheat on the final liquidation -- order of\n$200-350 against a ~$150k season. The other two gaps cost the opening and 551 unit ops. Recorded\nhere so the trade is visible rather than forgotten.\n\n## The fix, two options (as considered at the time)\n\n1. **Repeat the animal pair per unit in `bundle_of`, as `HIRE` already is.** `COW 2` becomes two\n   `(BUY_ANIMAL, COW)` pairs, and `to_sim_fields`'s `+= qty` with the default of 1 then yields 2.\n   No architecture change, no new head; `from_sim_fields` needs the matching `* n` so the two stay\n   inverses, and `tests/test_a_market_bundle_survives_the_round_trip.py` covers that. `max_held` is\n   6, so the vocabulary grows by a handful of entries at most.\n2. **Add a `qty_buy_animal` head.** Symmetric with the other three, but it is a shape change: every\n   checkpoint needs migrating and the head has to be trained from scratch.\n\n(1) is cheaper, matches the existing treatment of `HIRE`, and needs no checkpoint surgery.\n","numLines":164,"startLine":1,"totalLines":164}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d9381b91-4553-49d9-8d4d-5d830e4f2676","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/alexchilton/DataspellProjects/kaggriculture","sessionId":"1ceba71d-ed80-4dee-9d20-d544a8b98ee0","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"representation-can-say-what-the-teacher-does","slug":"iridescent-coalescing-gem"}

Here are the answers with file:line citations (line numbers are the file's own line numbers, from the `cat -n`-style output).\n\n## 1. Does any doc claim milk is \"floored at $1-$5\" or that we flood our own book?\n\nYes, explicitly, in **`docs/market_and_economics.md:68`**:\n\n> \"The forum claim 'multiply base price by shop count' is half right: shop count sets the sustainable drain *rate*, not the price — milk with three shops still floors after 76 units of excess, **which is why our own milk repeatedly closed at $1-$5** despite the claim saying it 'sells above 160 most of the season.'\" — sourced to `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`\n\nBacking measurements (same corpus, same date, all small-sample, real-replay-only):\n- `docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:20`: \"Real ladder losses... all three games ran herd COW 9-10 / SHEEP 4, and MILK floored in all three ($3, $5, $1) — 'that is not a seed, it is 9-10 cows.'\" (`history/three_ladder_games_2026-08-15.md`) — this is the closest thing to a \"we flood our own book\" claim: it pins the floor on our own oversized cow herd, not the market.\n- `docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:38-39`: two named games, `93239838` (\"MILK drained only 179 units and still floored at $3, base $160\") and `93242536` (\"MILK floored anyway at $1 despite 537 units drained\").\n- `docs/market_and_economics.md:13-14`: \"Units above I0 needed to reach the $1 floor: ... MILK 76\" and \"MILK 327/floors at 76\" (`history/the_book_is_shallow_2026-08-09.md`, `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`) — the mechanism, not a per-game outcome.\n\n**Corpus/date**: 3-5 individually read real ladder-loss replays, dated 2026-08-13 to 2026-08-15, explicitly caveated elsewhere as \"read off replays only, not simulation... nothing here is a win rate\" (`animals_and_the_herd.md:20`).\n\n**Does your 100-game table contradict it?** Not head-on — you're measuring different things. The docs' floor claims are anecdotes pulled from a handful of *losing* games (selection bias toward the failure mode), reported as closing/floor price in that specific game. Your table is a mean realized $/unit across 100 games (wins and losses mixed) using fill-deltas — MILK comes out at $93.9/unit, ours vs $97.6 theirs (96%), nowhere near a $1-$5 floor on average. Also worth noting: your fill-delta methodology matches the project's own corrected measurement standard — `market_and_economics.md:65` (\"100-109% of everything produced gets sold... the false reading came from comparing raw counts... and sampling at dusk\") and `animals_and_the_herd.md:14` (opponent volumes from OFFERS inflate ~6x vs fills) both argue fills, not offers/counts, are the right unit. So your number is credible and larger-sample, but it doesn't disprove that milk floors in *some* games — it shows the $1-$5 floor is not the modal or average outcome across 100 real games. I'd flag this for reconciliation rather than call the doc simply wrong.\n\nAlso relevant/older and now smaller-sample-superseded: `market_and_economics.md:16`, \"Realised prices, 12 recent ladder games: our wins sell MILK at $205/unit... our losses... $73\" (`history/the_market_2026-08-09.md`) — your $93.9 average sits inside that win/loss spread but is a materially larger sample (100 vs 12 games).\n\n## 2. CARROT verdict\n\nSettled as a **conditional win**, not a loss, not unresolved (per the doc's own framing) — but the gate width was refined, not the direction.\n\n- `docs/carrot_2026-08-22.md:1-9`: \"Carrot is not an open question. It is settled and thin, and the sweeps meant to thicken it produced zero rows.\" Cites `docs/status_2026-08-22.md`'s SETTLED conditional-rule table (lines 15-23): `town CA>=4, opp CA 1-14`, n=125, mean +1,143, 75% improved; vs mirror n=25 mean +1,659; vs `opp CA=0` n=100 mean +273; vs `opp CA 15+` n=25 mean **-510**, 28% improved; unconditional is **-$237** (losing towns cancel winning ones); fires ~1 game in 9, worth ~+$210/game. Own caveat quoted at line 24: \"**That figure rests on 3 towns and 57 seasons — thin.**\"\n- New evidence in the same file (`carrot_2026-08-22.md:58-114`): 6,048 seasons, 48 stratified towns x 9 opponents x 3 seeds, control arm 1,008 seasons — win% falls monotonically as town carrot-drain rises (73.0% at drain 0 down to 42.0% at drain 5), and this survives a substitution-artifact check (line 92-106: controlling for demand for our own crops moves win% only +1.5pts, carrot demand alone costs -10.1 to -15.0pts).\n- `docs/carrot_refine_2026-08-22.md`: the direct sweep, **48 towns x 7 opponents x 3 seeds = 1,008 paired seasons/arm**, 12,096 seasons total, opponents = 7 leaderboard tapes, all melon openers. Result (lines 27-39): control 54.7% win vs `cond_carrot_d5_o7` **60.0% win, McNemar net +55 flips to win / 0 to loss, z~7.4** (line 52-54). Value concentrates at `drain>=5` (lines 68-83: drain 5 delta +9.1pts, drain 6 +13.6pts, drain 7 +20.6pts; drain 4 is \"a wash,\" +1.2pts). Recommendation (line 125): **ship at `drain_min=5, opp_tiles_max=7`**, not the docstring's 4.\n- The static, unconditional swap is a documented **loss**: `docs/carrot_2026-08-22.md:49-53`, `docs/REGISTER.md` C-23 (`SHOP_ADAPT_CROPS`, `WHEAT<->CARROT`) measured 0.806 vs HEAD's 0.871 over 232 matched games, turned off 2026-08-11, \"register marks it UNVERIFIED, not re-affirmed\" — and that number predates the `c99a8a5` fix (2026-08-13) that made carrot sellable at all, so it's on a \"void number\" per the doc's own language (lines 49, 126-130).\n\n**Recorded reason both with- and without-carrot logic exist on the ladder**: `SHOP_ADAPT_CROPS = False` (unconditional swap, correctly off) is a separate flag from the new conditional rule `carrot_when_it_pays` (`kagg/search/tape_edit.py`), which as of `carrot_refine_2026-08-22.md:127-130` was **not yet in the shipped module list** (`scripts/build_planner_submission.py::MODULES`) — \"Inlining the rule there is preferable to adding a module to the bundle.\" So the coexistence is: old unconditional flag correctly off, new conditional rule proven but (per that doc) not yet inlined into the shipped `schedule_agent.py`.\n\nOpen item still flagged: whether `drain_min=6` beats 5 (untested), and the opponent-tile gate (`opp_tiles_max`) \"never binds on this opponent field\" because all 7 test opponents were melon openers with ≤3 standing carrot tiles (`carrot_refine_2026-08-22.md:136-147`) — real opponent carrot behavior only checked via 85 old replays (line 100-121), 94% zero carrot at d25, and those replays predate the 2026-08-15 hinge balance change, \"not checked\" whether appetite shifted.\n\n## 3. WOOL/sheep and MILK/cows\n\n- Herd sizes cluster (COW,SHEEP) = (6,8) in 9/14 games, (10,4) in 5/14 (`docs/market_and_economics.md:69`, `history/market_depth_and_the_morning_losses_2026-08-13.md`).\n- `WOOL_SHOP_SETS_THE_HERD` correctly caps cows at 6 on a 3-YARN_STORE board, but a bug in `_herd_split`'s early return under-delivers sheep (target COW6/SHEEP12=18 landed as COW6/SHEEP8=14, \"four animals short\") — `docs/market_and_economics.md:79`, `docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:40`.\n- Herd-split *scoring method* itself was corrected (scored at decision time, not final census), which alone moved match rate 11.5% → 55.8% → 65.6% → 77.9%, before any new rule; a `MILK_SHOPS_FOR_A_BIG_HERD=2` threshold then pushed it to 88.5% (`docs/market_and_economics.md:41`).\n- **Recorded reversal**: \"The prior 'opponent sheep count predicts our split' lead was pseudo-replication (213 placements over ~50 games); re-measured at the game level the gap collapses to -0.05 to -0.32, noise.\" (`docs/market_and_economics.md:42`, `history/the_greedy_is_a_mix_scorer_2026-08-20.md`)\n- Sell cadence/volume: \"Less, not slower\" — WOOL is called out as \"a pure volume problem (one episode: $5,278 observed vs $23,794 at 0.7x volume)\" (`docs/market_and_economics.md:73`); optimal sell fraction sweep found WOOL 0.7x best in 7/14 games, and re-pricing WOOL alone recovers \"+$135,250 summed\" across 14 losses (`docs/market_and_economics.md:20-21`).\n- Milk vs wool market structure: \"MILK realised price is board-driven... WOOL is opponent-driven... WOOL is sold by 1 of 8 shops (thin demand, opponent volume dominates) while MILK is in 3 of 8 (the draw dominates).\" (`docs/market_and_economics.md:64`). Wool's expected first demand day is 13.5 vs milk's 6.8, so \"a sheep bought early is money asleep\" (`docs/economics_q5_findings.md:88-89`).\n- Does the chain pay: \"Animals are the only uncapped producer, and they convert wheat into a product worth 4-8x the wheat. Milk at $344 with zero player sales is the strongest premium position on the board.\" (`docs/economics_q5_findings.md:251-252`) — with an explicit note that \"animals do not pay\" has been found to be an artifact of our own code **four separate times** (line 253-254, pointing to `experiment_log.md`, not one of the six files read).\n- Wool problem confirmed but non-decisive twice more: `docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:25` — WOOL floored at $1.00 in both of two later loss games (zero YARN_STORE drawn, we still ran 4 sheep), but \"BOTH seats made the same mistake in one game... so it is not the differentiator there.\"\n\nTangential but relevant infrastructure bug, different subsystem (the imitation-learning/DAgger pipeline, not the heuristic ladder agent): `docs/the_second_cow_cannot_be_bought_2026-08-22.md:1,26-54` — the bundle encoder could only ever express `BUY_ANIMAL COW/SHEEP` quantity 1, silently dropping `BUY_ANIMAL COW 2`/`SHEEP 2` (a $900 gap observed at turn 1, `docs/the_second_cow_cannot_be_bought_2026-08-22.md:35`), which desynchronized the whole crew's routing. This is not a reversal of a herd-size verdict — it's a policy-training encoding defect, fixed by repeating the animal pair per unit in `bundle_of` (lines 122-131, 153-163). Worth knowing if any of your 100 games were played by an IL-trained checkpoint rather than the heuristic `schedule_agent.py`.\n\n## 4. EGG and TOMATO — deliberate vs bug/gap\n\nBoth, depending on which:\n\n- **EGG = deliberately not farmed** (policy choice, not a gap): the closing geese audit concludes \"zero geese (-$11,758 margin expectation per six animals)\" and states `HERD_GEESE` deletion was correct (`docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:17-19`, `history/fable_geese_audit_2026-08-15.md`). Since EGG requires geese, EGG=0 in your table is exactly what the shipped policy intends.\n- **EGG/TOMATO/CARROT were also a hard *bug* until 2026-08-13**: \"`SELLABLE` listed only six of nine products — CARROT, TOMATO and EGG could not be sold at all — so every geese/eggs sweep before commit `c99a8a5` measured pure cost against zero revenue... not evidence about geese.\" (`docs/animals_and_the_herd.md:13`). This is now fixed and is a separate issue from the deliberate zero-geese policy.\n- **TOMATO is deliberately gated, not banned**: \"TOMATO is gated at `TOMATO_DRAIN_SHOPS_NEEDED=2`: 0 of 34 games with fewer than two tomato-draining shops ever planted a tomato tile.\" (`docs/market_and_economics.md:43`). A conditional tomato play is modeled to fire in \"~21-26% of games\" with expected value \"~$900/game mean\" (`docs/market_and_economics.md:27`).\n- Separately, `docs/market_and_economics.md:31` claims \"TOMATO and EGG were sold by literally 0 of 120 leader seats, the genuinely empty books\" (`history/carrot_and_the_empty_book_2026-08-19.md`).\n\nYour table shows TOMATO=0 for us across all 100 games too — consistent with the \"empty book\" framing for our side. But it also shows **opponents sold 136 TOMATO units** (at $105.4/u), which is inconsistent with \"literally 0 of 120 leader seats\" — see Q5 below, this is a candidate contradiction.\n\n## 5. What your table makes stale or wrong\n\n1. **`docs/market_and_economics.md:68`** — \"our own milk repeatedly closed at $1-$5\" — your 100-game aggregate ($93.9/u ours, 96% of opponents' $97.6/u) shows this is not the modal/average outcome. Not strictly \"wrong\" (it's an anecdote from specific losses), but presented as a general pattern it's now stale against a 100-game measurement and should be re-scoped to \"in some cow-heavy losses\" rather than \"repeatedly.\" Flag for correction.\n\n2. **`docs/market_and_economics.md:31`** — \"TOMATO and EGG were sold by literally 0 of 120 leader seats, the genuinely empty books.\" Your table shows opponents sold **136 TOMATO units at $105.4/unit** across 100 games (EGG still 0 both sides, so that half stands).
